page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82334/

AI cloud providers are under pressure to deploy next‑generation AI infrastructure faster, more efficiently, and at multi‑gigawatt scale. ### AI Grid Tokenomics: Powering Distributed AI Inference at Scale
Speakers: Andy Champagne (Akamai), Chris Penrose (NVIDIA), Nader Foroughi (Comcast)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82012/

As AI usage explodes, optimizing economics and performance of inference is becoming just as critical as training the models themselves. A distributed AI grid makes tokenomics radically more efficient—improving cost per token, time-to-first-token, and token throughput while delivering deterministic performance for the applications that need it most, including highly interactive and latency-sensitive experiences. In this session, two companies advancing AI grids will share which applications they prioritize for distributed versus centralized deployments and how key workload key performance indicators (KPIs) drive those choices. See concrete examples, benchmark results, and economic models that illustrate how NVIDIA-powered distributed infrastructure can enable “least-cost, best-performance” serving at scale—and what that unlocks for the next wave of AI services and business models.

NVIDIA CUDA Direct Sparse Solver (cuDSS) is increasingly being adopted in large-scale simulations for engineering and design, such as those pertinent to electronic engineering automation (EDA) and computer-aided engineering (CAE). In this session, we will cover recent advances, including most recent performance number, highlights of key features, and new APIs in cuDSS that enable users across various workflows to leverage GPU acceleration for sparse solvers.

By deep-diving into the QCT Dev Kit architecture, a toolkit that integrates high-performance QCT GPU servers with NVIDIA Cosmos, NVIDIA Isaac GR00T, and NVIDIA Omniverse, customers can deliver end-to-end physical AI solutions faster than ever. Learn how QCT works with TM Robot to overcome humanoid era challenges and achieve industrial workload validation. Through the TM Robot "Xplore I" collaboration, the companies manage to improve humanoid performance via specialized GUI designs and enable five key features (system, skills, datasets, models, and USD file management).

### Activate Always-On AI Defense Across Local and Enterprise Agents
Speakers: Bartley Richardson (NVIDIA), Mike Petronaci (CrowdStrike)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81765/

AI is rapidly transforming the cybersecurity landscape, with security operations centers (SOCs) facing growing AI-powered threats that overwhelm traditional tools and workflows. AI-driven defense is therefore no longer optional—it is a necessity. Organizations are not only re-architecting their SOCs but also their infrastructure, with the transition to AI factories. This session explores how organizations can move from experimentation to secure deployment of AI agents using an agentic security platform that streamlines analyst workflows and orchestrates adaptive defenses across dynamic environments. Learn how CrowdStrike’s agentic cybersecurity platform integrates with NVIDIA’s AI stack to secure AI across the enterprise, building a resilient foundation for AI-driven cyber defense at enterprise scale.

### Best Practices for Scaling Inference
Speakers: Jalaj Thanaki (NVIDIA), Anish Mukherjee (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81519/

As organizations scale toward population-level AI adoption, running inference reliably, efficiently, and cost-effectively becomes essential. This session covers best practices for scaling inference across platform, model, and application layers, using real-world insights and NVIDIA technologies like DGXC-Lepton and NVCF. We’ll address challenges in GPU capacity, autoscaling, model optimization with NVIDIA S/W stack, concurrency, architecture, and observability. Learn how to use proven, field-tested strategies for scaling current AI workloads or launching new ones at scale.

Speakers: Ely Greenfield (Adobe)

Learn how to design, train, and deploy large-scale custom generative AI models. This session will step you through data preparation, fine-tuning, and infrastructure management, and will highlight best practices for scaling large models efficiently. You’ll also see real-world examples with Adobe Firefly Foundry and NVIDIA NeMo integration that demonstrate how teams are applying these techniques to production-grade AI systems.

### Building Domain-Expert Agents: How to Optimize Txt2SQL and Tool-Calling with Open Models
Speakers: Santiago Pombo (NVIDIA), Arijit Sengupta (Aible), Rockford Yost (Yum! Brands)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81707/
Type: Talk
Enterprises are moving beyond prompt engineering toward repeatable, data-driven customization loops. This technical session explores how Aible and Yum! Brands transitioned NVIDIA Nemotron 3 from general-purpose models into specialized domain experts. Aible demonstrates a Txt2SQL data flywheel designed for highly regulated environments, utilizing SME feedback and data augmentation to drive query accuracy and strict compliance. Yum! Brands details their approach to agentic tool-calling for high-volume ordering (e.g., Taco Bell), leveraging Small Language Models (SLMs) and SDG-style reasoning traces to improve tool-use disambiguation and execution latency. Arijit Sengupta (CEO & Founder, Aible), Rocky Yost (Head of AI, Yum! Brands) and NVIDIA walk through these production data flywheels built with NVIDIA NeMo microservices. Learn how to design evaluation-led iteration cycles and determine when to use specialized SLMs versus full-scale model alignment for your own domain-specific use cases.

### Category Theory in Commodities: Applying Sheaf-Based Graph Neural Networks and Quantum Portfolio Optimization
Speakers: Yigal Jhirad (Cohen & Steers), Emanuel Scoullos (NVIDIA), Siddharth Samsi (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82403/
Type: Talk
We apply sheaf neural networks, a generalization of graph neural networks (GNNs), to model commodities and capture cross-curve, cross-sector, and macro linkages. These models are used to generate probabilistic forecasts of returns, volatility, and co-movements. The resulting signals can then be combined with quantum-based portfolio optimization techniques to explore risk–return trade-offs.

Speakers: Rachel Allen (NVIDIA), Roopa Prabhu (NVIDIA)

You’ll leave with frameworks and real-world examples to help scale knowledge, efficiency, and impact—no matter your role or industry.

### How AI Factories Can Shift-left to Accelerate Infrastructure and Ecosystems Readiness
Speakers: Amit Katz (NVIDIA), Harshdeep Banwait (CoreWeave)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82176/
Type: Talk
As AI infrastructure scales, the traditional “build-then-test” model has become a critical bottleneck. Join NVIDIA and CoreWeave to explore how a shift-left methodology, powered by digital twins, is transforming the AI factory lifecycle. CoreWeave will share how NVIDIA Air decouples software automation from hardware lead times, enabling validation of complex systems well before physical infrastructure arrives. In parallel, NVIDIA uses these digital twin environments to empower a global ecosystem of software partners to develop and optimize next-generation platforms without requiring access to physical hardware. This session demonstrates how a simulation-first approach ensures the entire stack, from cloud automation to ISV solutions, is production-ready on day one, dramatically reducing time-to-market and capital risk.

### Insights From NVIDIA Research
Speakers: Bill Dally (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82168/

The talk will give some highlights from NVIDIA Research for the past year. Detailed topics will be disclosed closer to the event.

### Massive GPU Acceleration for Scalable Quantum Compilation and Graph Analytics
Speakers: Oded Green (NVIDIA), Yulun Wang (Q-CTRL)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81533/
Type: Talk
Subgraph isomorphism is a major bottleneck in graph analytics, scientific computing, and quantum-compilation pipelines, yet most solutions rely on backtracking that cannot exploit modern GPUs. Motivated by real quantum-compilation challenges, we present a GPU-native formulation that replaces serial search with parallel relational tabular operations built from reusable “motifs,” enabling efficient joins and filters using NVIDIA RAPIDS. Across diverse benchmarks, we achieve up to 600x speedups on a single NVIDIA H200 GPU. By combining Q-CTRL’s quantum performance management infrastructure software and NVIDIA’s GPU platforms, we demonstrate a data-centric method that simplifies high-performance graph analysis, accelerates large-scale applications, and opens a path toward emerging hybrid HPC–quantum architectures and future deployment on HPC–quantum interconnect systems like NVQLink.

### Megatron Core: Customer Stories on the Open-Source Training Framework for Ultra Speed at Scale
Speakers: Costa Huang (Periodic Labs), Santosh Bhavani (NVIDIA), Wang Zhang (ByteDance Seed), Aakanksha Chowdhery (Reflection AI), Hongbin Liu (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82214/
Type: Lightning Talk

You will also hear about their contributions into this OSS project, features they leveraged for NVIDIA's latest hardware systems, and tuning for best performance at scale.

### Optimize KV Caches for LLM Inference: Dynamo KVBM, FlexKV,  LMCache
Speakers: Junchen Jiang (Tensormesh), Ziqi Fan (NVIDIA), Vikram Sharma Mailthody (NVIDIA), Chao Pei (Tencent)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82033/
Type: Tutorial
This tutorial provides a practitioner-led deep dive into why KV-cache design is a first-order performance lever for production LLM inference, directly impacting throughput, tail latency, and GPU utilization. Drawing on real production experience, the session walks through KV-cache design principles and implementation approaches used in FlexKV, LMCache, and Dynamo KVBM. The discussion highlights practical lessons from deploying KV caching with modern attention patterns such as MQA, GQA, DS3.2, and speculative decoding. You'll leave with actionable recipes, code patterns, and profiling techniques to integrate KV caching into existing inference engines and scale beyond a single node. Special thanks to Fan Ye, who is the initiator of the open-source project FlexKV and contributed most of the FlexKV part of this session.

page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81681/

By operating a continuous “digital twin-to-real” loop, AUDI can monitor factory operations in near-real time, simulate changes before deployment, and rapidly adapt to new product variants or process adjustments. NVIDIA’s industrial platforms—such as Omniverse for factory-scale digital twins, Isaac for robotics AI workflows, and NVIDIA’s edge and data center AI systems—provide the compute, simulation, and optimization capabilities making this production model feasible and scalable.

We’ll cover Factory Playback solutions for creating a searchable "time machine" across production and warehouse operations and associated ROI. By synchronizing operational events with multi-camera video, data, and workflows, teams gain verifiable visual context to enforce standard operating procedures (SOPs), accelerate root-cause analysis, and automate responses to process deviations.

### How Simulation and AI are Giving Life to Digital Twins for Patient Care

These comprehensive simulations are already being used to conduct virtual clinical trials and train autonomous surgical robots. We're helping to build the future in which AI is used to design, accelerate, and interpret these simulations, enabling digital twins that guide the care of each and every patient. Throughout the day, as your smart watch records your activities or during surgical procedures when unexpected events occur, your AI-enabled digital twin will be there to detect, diagnose, and suggest the treatment that provides the best outcome for you.

### Securing Coding Assistants
Speakers: Rich Harang (NVIDIA), Tom Daniels (Cursor), Henry Wildermuth (Cursor), Becca Lynch (ThreatOps) (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81493/
Type: Talk
As AI-powered coding assistants become more capable—and begin interacting directly with developer environments—their security risks grow alongside their utility. This session offers a practical demonstration of how vulnerabilities can emerge when these assistants gain computer-use capabilities, exposing new attack surfaces inside the software development life cycle. We then dive into Cursor’s real-world mitigation strategies, showing how hard security controls around critical functions create a flexible balance between capability and risk. Finally, we connect lessons from AI security research, secure application design, and red-teaming exercises to illustrate how organizations can safely unlock higher levels of automation. Together, these practices chart a path toward powerful AI development tools with far lower operational risk.

### Speed Meets Safety: Accelerating Automotive Crash Simulations With AI Physics
Speakers: Sudeep Chavare (General Motors), Srinivas Tadepalli (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81785/
Type: Talk
Traditional automotive crashworthiness assessment relies on computationally expensive finite element simulations, creating a bottleneck in automotive design. This work with General Motors introduces machine learning surrogate models, utilizing MeshGraphNet and Transolver architectures within the NVIDIA PhysicsNeMo framework. Evaluated on a Body-in-White dataset of 150 simulations, the study compares time-conditional and stability-enhanced autoregressive modeling strategies. The models successfully predict spatiotemporal deformation trends using undeformed geometry and variable thickness inputs. Although full finite element accuracy remains out of reach, the approach achieves massive computational speedups. These results demonstrate the feasibility of ML-driven dynamics for rapid, early-stage crashworthiness optimization.

### Use Reinforcement Learning to Turn AI Agents Into Continuous Learners
Speakers: Kang Li (Atlassian), Robert Van Dusen (Google)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82359/
Type: Talk
Static prompt engineering breaks under real-world complexity. This session shows how Reinforcement Learning (RL) enables agents to make better decisions, use tools reliably, and adapt in production. This talk distills lessons from Atlassian on how it built Rovo, its enterprise AI solution for search, AI chat, and agents. The team explains how it moved from static prompt engineering to policy training via RL thereby improving intent interpretation, retrieval quality and how they use NVIDIA NeMo RL to evolve query rewriting from brittle rules to adaptive learning. The session then expands to platform RL, where Google Cloud Vertex Training Cluster showcases how its integration of NVIDIA NeMo libraries has helped customers build best-in-class models. The team will deep dive on Vertex’s managed GPU infrastructure and how NeMo RL and other techniques can be used to build high performance agents."

### Build Trust and Discovery Through Open‑Source AI in Research
Speakers: Percy Liang (Co-Founder, Together AI, Co-Founder, Simile AI), Hanna Hajishirzi (AI2)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81835/

Professor Percy Liang will introduce Marin, an open lab for frontier AI where experiments happen in public, with community review and contributions via GitHub, advancing reproducibility, benchmarking, and data-efficient training. ### Extend Robot Perception With Industrial Safety Agents
Speakers: Johannes Hinckeldeyn (KION Group)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81838/
Type: Talk
Autonomous forklifts and mobile robots are transforming loading docks and warehouse aisles, but relying only on onboard sensing leaves them vulnerable to blind spots, occlusions, and conservative safety behaviors that increase cost. KION will show how they use NVIDIA Halos for outside-in safety on NVIDIA IGX, combining infrastructure cameras, low-latency detections, and safety logic to supervise multiple robots with virtual fences, tripwires, and dynamic zones. Learn how outside-in safety architecture boosts throughput, reduces false stops, and enables closer human–robot collaboration for use cases such as autonomous forklift trailer loading and autonomous mobile robot (AMR) zoning with Metropolis VSS and Cosmos Reason.

Expect rapid insights, vivid demonstrations, and forward-looking ideas that reveal where generative and agentic AI are heading—and what they make possible today.

Hear from Perplexity AI on why they have adopted MoE models, detailing how they leverage Blackwell NVL72 to deliver real-time search with breakthrough performance.

page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82227/

Gain insight into Supermicro's deployment of these platforms as validated, rack-scale solutions, rather than standalone servers. We'll cover key design and deployment considerations, including direct-to-chip cooling and rack-level liquid integration, and show how liquid-cooled total solutions accelerate time-to-online while improving performance and energy efficiency for modern AI factories.

### MLOps 202: From Models to Production AI Systems at Scale
Speakers: Nik Spirin (NVIDIA), Erik Bohnhorst (NVIDIA), Michael Balint (NVIDIA)

Modern production AI is more than a foundation model. The model monolith is evolving into the data center-scale distributed system made of modular microservices. This talk shows how NVIDIA brings the full stack together by applying extreme software and hardware co-design to maximize performance across infrastructure, orchestration, and application layers. We cover an AI project journey from zero to production. Starting with a simple cloud API, we progressively add complexity while introducing key NVIDIA technologies, including NVIDIA AI Enterprise, NIM, NeMo, Run.ai, Dynamo, and GPU infrastructure, to explain how each of them addresses real engineering and operational challenges. Get a clear guide for turning AI prototypes and isolated experiments into enterprise-grade systems, and understand the decisions, trade-offs, and architectural patterns behind modern production AI.

### Smart Agents, Safer Systems: Engineering AI for Trust and Transparency
Speakers: Kunal Anand (F5, Inc.), Ash Bhalgat (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81841/
Type: Talk
As autonomous AI agents expand enterprise automation, they introduce a growing attack surface that traditional security controls were not designed to address. This session shows why visibility and policy enforcement in the AI factory are critical for trust, and how GPU-accelerated cyber models act as intelligent guardrails against threats such as prompt injection, model exfiltration, and data leakage. Through examples and an architectural walkthrough, we demonstrate a full-stack solution using NVIDIA NIM-based inference, NVIDIA Zero Trust Networking, the NVIDIA Morpheus SDK, DPU-accelerated firewalls, and F5 AI Guardrails. Learn how to offload security functions into an isolated trust domain to ensure zero-trust enforcement without compromising workload performance, and leave with an actionable blueprint for SecOps and AI teams to turn AI risk into a competitive differentiator.

### Sovereign AI for Telcos: Offtake Stories and Monetization Models From Around the World
Speakers: Joao Kluck Gomes (NVIDIA), Chris Madan (TELUS), Vikram Sinha (Indosat Ooredoo Hutchison (IOH)), Ahmed El Beheiry (Cassava AI), Min-Yong Ha (SK Telecom)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82019/

Sovereign AI is rapidly becoming a new growth engine for telcos as they build national AI clouds and monetize them in very different ways. This panel brings together leading operators from across the world to share how they are turning NVIDIA-powered sovereign AI infrastructure into real revenue and real impact for their nations. Panelists will share their customer stories and discuss a variety of business models. They will also highlight how innovation hubs are enabling local developers, startups, and enterprises to build country-specific AI applications. The session will close with concrete lessons learned, demand generation, and a call to action for telcos and app builders looking to participate in the sovereign AI opportunity.

### Supercharge LLM Inference With Dynamo
Speakers: Harry Kim (NVIDIA), Kyle Kranen (NVIDIA), David Lu (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81762/
Type: Tutorial
NVIDIA Dynamo is a distributed inference serving framework built to run large language models efficiently, reliably, and at massive scale. It enables AI providers to reduce cost-per-token, meet strict service-level objectives, and scale deployments across large GPU clusters. In this interactive session, you’ll learn how Dynamo delivers high-performance distributed inference across frameworks such as SGLang, TensorRT-LLM, and vLLM. We’ll cover how to compose KV-aware routing, disaggregated serving, and KV offloading to achieve the most optimal throughput and latency given available GPU resources. Additionally, we will detail how to configure Dynamo deployment for Kubernetes (K8s) with topology-aware hierarchical gang scheduling, enable auto-scaling to adapt to real-time changes in traffic, and ensure reliability with fine-grained LLM fault tolerance.

### The AI-XR Co-Scientist Lab: How Stanford - Princeton's LabOS is Accelerating Advanced Science With XR
Speakers: Le Cong (Stanford University), Mengdi Wang (Princeton University)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81748/
Type: Talk
What if we could dramatically shorten the path from discovery to lifesaving therapy? Discover how Stanford Medicine’s LabOS, an AI extended reality (XR) operating system, makes this a reality. Learn how LabOS drives CRISPR GPT, a groundbreaking AI agent to design complex experiments and deliver step-by-step instructions through augmented reality glasses. LabOS unites computational reasoning with physical experimentation, built on NVIDIA Cosmos-powered XR/AI system. By connecting multi-model AI agents, smart glasses, and robots, LabOS allows AI to see what scientists see, understand experimental context, and assist in real-time execution. From immunotherapy to stem cell engineering, LabOS turns any lab into an intelligent environment where human and machine discovery evolve together—not just accelerating research, but delivering personalized, accessible therapies at unprecedented scale.

### Transforming Bottlenecks into Breakthroughs: Scaling Secure AI Networks for AI Factories (Presented by Cisco Systems)

page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82187/

Cisco and NVIDIA tackle bandwidth, latency, and security challenges head-on with innovations in silicon, systems, software, and optics. Scale fabric architectures to maximize AI workloads, ensuring efficient expansion and secure data in motion. Explore validated architectures that simplify deployment, optimize GPU use, and future-proof AI infrastructure. Chart your path to breakthroughs.

### Vibe Decoding Quantum Error Correction With CUDA-Q
Speakers: Joschka Roffe (University of Edinburgh)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81722/
Type: Talk
Any fault-tolerant quantum computer must be tightly integrated with classical co-processors that decode quantum error correction measurements in real time. These decoders must operate with extreme speed, high accuracy, and broad versatility. In this talk, I will present our recently proposed Vibe Decoding strategy, in which an ensemble of diverse decoders is run in parallel to achieve performance beyond current state-of-the-art approaches. I'll also discuss its practical realization using CUDA-Q.

### Voice-Native Agents: Rearchitecting Conversational AI with Open, Low-Latency Speech Models
Speakers: Rockford Yost (Yum! Brands), Oluwatobi Olabiyi (NVIDIA)

In this hands-on tutorial, two lead engineers walk through how to build real-time, voice-native conversational systems using integrated open-weight models and response generation. Learn when to open models to power low-latency, natural conversations in production. You’ll get practical guidance on customizing Nemotron Speech for your domain, wiring in function calling, and adapting models quickly to new intents, accents, and acoustic environments. We also explore how to use tools like Data Designer to tame fragmented or franchise data sources and turn them into robust, queryable pipelines for conversational AI. Through live coding and demos, you'll see how to stitch together end-to-end pipelines for high-value scenarios so you can develop voice agents with low latency and optimal efficiency at scale.

### No One Will Use Your AI Agents if They Don’t Feel Human (Presented by ElevenLabs)

page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82315/

AI agents are getting smarter—but many still fail to earn real usage because they don’t feel human in live interaction. In this session, we’ll explore why agents need a conversational interface, what it takes to handle real-time interactions at scale, and how to build brand trust and repeat usage.

### A Deep Dive Into Two Telco-Built Sovereign AI Clouds
Speakers: Bill Chang (SingTel), Peter Lorenz (Deutsche Telekom, T-Systems International)

More than 20 telcos worldwide have already built sovereign AI infrastructure powered by NVIDIA, with many more underway. This session brings together two leading operators who will dive deep into their vision and journey for sovereign AI in their markets: how they designed and built their infrastructure, what they are optimized for, how adoption is ramping, and where their roadmaps are headed. Gain a front-row view into how telcos are turning sovereign AI into real platforms for innovation, growth, and national resilience.

### AI Factory in Action:  Achieving Agile Automation With Gen AI Agents and Digital Twins (Presented by ASUS)
Speakers: Joseph Lu (ASUS), Jesse Chen (Spingence Technology)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82038/
Type: Talk
As manufacturing shifts from mass production to high-mix, low-volume demands, flexibility becomes key. Traditional automation faces a critical bottleneck: rigidity. We'll explore the paradigm shift toward the "AI factory," a facility where intelligence is manufactured alongside products. ASUS will present a full-stack architecture with our solution partners that integrates agentic AI—powered by generative AI and vision language models—with digital twins built on NVIDIA Omniverse libraries and OpenUSD. Discover how to build a high-performance, closed-loop system where AI agents perceive changes, reason through complex tasks, and validate strategies within a high-fidelity virtual environment using NVIDIA Isaac Sim before physical execution. By leveraging NVIDIA Omniverse for digital twins, manufacturers can simulate edge cases and optimize robotic workflows in a risk-free virtual space.

### Accelerate Oracle AI Database Embedding and Vector Index Creation With NVIDIA (Presented by Oracle)
Speakers: Weiwei Gong (Oracle), Tirthankar Lahiri (Oracle), Corey Nolet (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82390/
Type: Panel
As enterprise vector data grows, keeping vector indexes up to date becomes increasingly important for similarity search and retrieval-augmented generation (RAG). In partnership with NVIDIA, Oracle AI Database uses GPUs to accelerate vector workflows by speeding up large-scale embedding generation and vector index creation. With Oracle AI Database, users can run vector similarity search directly in SQL, as well as via APIs and SDKs. Join us for a walkthrough of using Oracle AI Database capabilities with NVIDIA GPUs, learn how they can reduce time-to-value for vector workloads, and see how Deutsche Bank is employing them.

### Architects of the Accelerated Age: How CUDA Builders Changed the World (and How You’re Next)
Speakers: Joe Stam (Jump Trading), Stephen Jones (NVIDIA), Kate Clark (NVIDIA), Paulius Micikevicius (Meta Superintelligence Labs), Wen-Mei Hwu (Senior Distinguished Research Scientist and Senior Research Director at NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82416/
Type: Panel
The world was changed by creators who wrote the first kernels and turned raw potential into reality. Now, they are sharing the blueprints. Join us for an unfiltered look at CUDA’s impact with prolific creators who have redefined the boundaries of their fields. Hear the "aha" moments and war stories behind the software that shifted society. Since 2007, CUDA has served as the foundation for an accelerated computing revolution, enabling breakthroughs from genomic decoding to generative AI. This scale wasn't built on "killer apps" alone, but through decades of continuous evolution: maturing compilers, expanding libraries, and simplifying the path from idea to deployment. From CUDA-X to accessibility leaps like tile, the platform has evolved alongside its builders. Explore the journey from the first line of code to global scale and leave ready to build your own world-changing legacy.

### Automating Vision and Reporting for Utility Inspections
Speakers: Chris Nielsen (Levatas), Kellen Kirk (PG&E)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81866/
Type: Talk
Pacific Gas and Electric (PG&E), one of the largest utility companies in the United States serving millions across Northern and Central California, is facing the same urgent grid modernization challenge as the broader energy sector—and is meeting it with NVIDIA’s physical AI stack. In this session, Levatas and PG&E will show how Cosmos Reason VLM, Metropolis, Blueprint for Video Search and Summarization (VSS), and synthetic data generation are being leveraged to transform reactive grid maintenance into predictive, automated asset inspection at national scale. Learn how PG&E is beginning to deliver rapid, high-accuracy assessments like thermal anomaly detection, segmentation-based corrosion detection, and analog gauge reading at 99% accuracy, while accelerating post-storm recovery and reducing downtime through automated, geo-tagged, time-stamped asset condition reporting.

### Beyond Guardrails: Best Practices Across the Industry for Trustworthy AI
Speakers: Nikki Pope (NVIDIA), Margaret Mitchell (Hugging Face)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81537/

### Build Next-Generation Foundation Models in Finance
Speakers: Tadas Krisciunas (Revolut)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81618/
Type: Talk
Financial transactions share key properties with language: temporal ordering, contextual dependencies, and patterns emerging from surrounding context. This session presents a framework for building transaction foundation models using masked prediction and next-item forecasting to learn rich representations of customer behavior without labeled data. We'll cover our end-to-end approach—from tokenizing transactions into sequences to self-supervised objectives capturing spending rhythms, cross-merchant correlations, and anomaly patterns. Learn how this architecture drives improvements across fraud detection, credit risk, and behavior prediction while drastically reducing labeled data requirements.

### Build and Deploy AI Models and Agents for Media & Entertainment
Speakers: Rick Champagne (NVIDIA), Ashlee Martino-Tarr (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82144/
Type: Talk
As artificial intelligence evolves from passive tools to active collaborators, a new paradigm is emerging: agentic AI. These autonomous, goal-driven systems—known as AI agents—are designed not just to answer questions, but to plan, act, and adapt in pursuit of creative objectives. In this session, we’ll demystify what AI agents are, how they work, and why they matter for artists, designers, storytellers, and technologists. From intelligent production assistants that automate repetitive tasks to co-creative agents, this talk explores real-world use cases across film, animation, gaming, and immersive media. We’ll also look under the hood at the frameworks used to build these agents and discuss how to keep humans in the loop—preserving creative intent and artistic control.

### Design an Enterprise-Grade Platform for Generative and Agentic AI at Scale (Presented by EXL Service)
Speakers: Aidan McGowran (Exlservice Holdings, Inc.)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82048/

In this session we explore EXLerate.ai, EXL’s enterprise-grade platform for developing, orchestrating, and running Gen AI and agentic AI solutions at scale. We will illustrate how EXLerate.ai enables capabilities such as domain-aware copilots, agentic workflow orchestration, intelligent automation, and decision intelligence in enterprise environments. While generative AI presents significant opportunities to transform how work gets done, it also introduces complex challenges related to enterprise data integration, contextual relevance, governance, security, cost efficiency, and operational reliability. EXLerate.ai addresses these challenges through a modular platform architecture, enterprise data and knowledge grounding, flexible model orchestration, and built-in guardrails designed to support responsible, scalable, and production-ready AI systems.

### From AI Factories to the Edge: Architecting Distributed Inference to Unlock Enterprise ROI (Presented by Akamai)
Speakers: Shawn Michels (Akamai)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82071/
Type: Talk
AI inference and agents are forcing infrastructure to evolve. As interaction shifts from human to machine-centric, unlocking latency, cost, and performance advantages will be critical. AI Factories excel at foundational model training and large-scale concurrent GPU workloads. However, production inference—especially multi-modal workloads and agentic workflows requiring low-latency responses, multi-step reasoning, and real-time tool calls—demands a different approach. This session demonstrates how distributed inference architectures optimize GPU utilization for responsiveness to maximize ROI by keeping training centralized while deploying fine-tuning, post-training, and production inference on high-performance global networks.

### How NASCAR Uses GPU-Accelerated Simulation for Safer, More Exciting Racing
Speakers: Neil Ashton (NVIDIA), Eric Jacuzzi (NASCAR)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81752/
Type: Talk
Go under the hood of NASCAR’s rigorous engineering workflow, where computational fluid dynamics (CFD) now accounts for 80% of all aerodynamic development. We'll explain how simulation drives critical decisions regarding driver safety, thermal management, and the racing package itself—specifically through "liftoff safety" studies and multi-car traffic modeling. This is made possible by NASCAR’s transition from CPU-based CFD to the CUDA-X accelerated Siemens Simcenter STAR-CCM+ package, in partnership with TotalSim and the Ohio Supercomputer Center. Leveraging the latest NVIDIA GB200 GPUs, this workflow delivers a 10x improvement in turnaround time, enabling larger, higher-fidelity multi-physics models.

### Leverage DL to Enable Blood-Based Multi-Cancer Detection and Empower Health Systems to Reach More Patients
Speakers: Riley Ennis (Freenome)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81820/
Type: Talk
Multi-modal liquid biopsy tests with paired clinical data have the potential to unlock deep learning (DL) models to improve population-level multi-cancer detection to reach the 120 million people in the United States alone who are eligible for cancer screening. Such tests produce data for millions of molecules, such as cell-free DNA (and billions of bases) per patient, before incorporating EMR, imaging, and other data types. Learn more about the unique challenges and opportunities that Freenome and others are collaborating on to validate, launch, and improve ML/DL models for multi-cancer, and ultimately multi-disease, detection.

### Performance Optimization of cuTile Kernels
Speakers: Vishal Mehta (NVIDIA), Rob Armstrong (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81705/
Type: Tutorial
This tutorial guides you through the implementation of Mixture of Experts (MoE) using cuTile, focusing on how to express complex dataflows—including matrix products, gather and scatter of tokens, dynamic routing—directly in terms of multidimensional tiles. Explore how the cuTile compiler automates the most difficult aspects of GPU optimization, such as asynchronous memory movement, software pipelining, and Tensor Core utilization. By the end of the session, you'll understand how to move from a high-level algorithm description to a production-quality kernel that achieves near-peak performance while remaining readable and maintainable.

### Physical AI at Scale: Turn Real-World Intelligence Into Enterprise Advantage (Presented by Tata Consultancy Services)
Speakers: Naresh Mehta (Tata Consultancy Services), Sreenivasa Chakravarti (Tata Consultancy Services)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82102/
Type: Talk
Physical AI moves enterprise intelligence into real-world systems that sense, reason, and act. Scaling from pilots to enterprise operations remains challenging. In this session, TCS and a global enterprise discuss how Physical AI is operationalized using NVIDIA accelerated computing to deliver measurable outcomes across physical operations. The session walks through a representative use case integrating Physical AI assets—combining real-time sensing, simulation, and decisioning using NVIDIA Omniverse for digital twin simulation, Isaac Sim for robotics and physical system modeling, NVIDIA Metropolis VSS Blueprint for video analytics AI agents, and NVIDIA NIM for optimized inference. Speakers share production learnings on integrating physical data streams and sustaining performance and reliability at scale. Attendees leave with a grounded view of how to scale Physical AI in production and turn real-world intelligence into enterprise advantage.

### Realizing the Promise of Quantum Computation
Speakers: William Oliver (Massachusetts Institute of Technology)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82295/
Type: Talk
Quantum computers are fundamentally different than conventional computers. They promise to address certain problems that are practically prohibitive, and even impossible, to solve using today’s supercomputers. The challenge is building one that is large enough to be useful. In this talk, we will provide an overview of contemporary quantum computing at an intuitive level, including the technology, the promise, the hype, the importance of high-performance classical computing in this endeavor, and the challenges ahead associated with realizing useful quantum computers at scale.

### Reinventing Security for AI at Scale
Speakers: Ofir Arkin (NVIDIA), Rich Campagna (Palo Alto Networks)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81691/
Type: Talk
As enterprises build AI factories, massive data flows, distributed compute, and real-time inferencing push infrastructure to new limits while introducing new security risks. An AI factory is a specialized data center built for intelligence at scale, yet traditional defenses struggle to secure dynamic AI pipelines and emergent model behavior without impacting performance. This session explores how a secure-by-design architecture powered by Palo Alto Networks AIRS and accelerated by NVIDIA BlueField delivers real-time, inline protection at the speed of AI. By embedding security controls at the infrastructure layer, organizations gain agentless visibility and enforcement across the AI life cycle. AIRS detects threats, blocks prompt-level attacks, and governs agent actions as systems scale. Together, these capabilities enable trustworthy, governed, high-throughput AI operations.

### Solving the Trust Dilemma by Ensuring Model and Data Privacy On Premises with Confidential AI
Speakers: Erik Bohnhorst (NVIDIA), Zvonko Kaiser (NVIDIA), Shaheen Lavie-Rouse (ElevenLabs)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82081/
Type: Talk
Regulated industries like healthcare and finance require running AI models locally for privacy and compliance, as moving up to 70% of sensitive enterprise data to the public cloud is costly and impractical. This necessitates bringing AI to the data. This creates a Trust Dilemma: Enterprises need strong guarantees that sensitive data remains private and inaccessible to external parties, while model providers need assurance that their proprietary models are protected when deployed outside their direct control. This dilemma often restricts enterprises to open-source models, with advanced proprietary models only available as SaaS. NVIDIA Confidential Computing resolves this by using trusted execution environments to protect both sensitive data and model intellectual property. We will showcase a reference architecture that securely deploys proprietary models into enterprise infrastructure without exposing models or data to operators or vendors. This approach allows AI providers to meet customers where their data resides, enabling enterprises to safely apply advanced AI to their most sensitive data.

### The AI-Augmented Surgeon: Inside the Operating Room of the Future
Speakers: Osamah Choudhry (Medivis), Edward Andrews (University of Pittsburgh Medical Center (UPMC))
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82005/
Type: Talk
Learn how Medivis is revolutionizing surgical care with the University of Pittsburgh Medical Center (UPMC) and MAIA, their groundbreaking extended reality (XR) and AI platform. Discover how MAIA gives surgeons real-time, conversational access to vast medical data through an advanced retrieval-augmented generation system. We'll also showcase its powerful image recognition capabilities powered by NVIDIA Nemo Agent Toolkit and Cosmos, delivering the next generation of surgical precision and care.

### The Edge of the Edge: Redefining GPU-Enabled AI Sensor Processing
Speakers: Adam Thompson (NVIDIA), John Ammerman (National Instruments), Felix Bachmair (DECTRIS), Giovanni Campanella (Texas Instruments), Paul Golding (Analog Devices)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81525/
Type: Lightning Talk
Physical AI and edge supercomputing are both concerned with the timely delivery and processing of physical measurements on GPU to build the future of autonomous decision-making across industries and domains—from scientific instruments to industrial robotics. During this talk, we will hear from leading sensor manufacturers who are building real-time AI solutions with NVIDIA and the Holoscan platform; they'll share their best practices and their vision, and highlight transformational work toward a future of AI-powered instruments.

### The New Compute Paradigm: Adapting Infrastructure for the Inference Era
Speakers: Sachin Katti (OpenAI)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82430/
Type: Talk
As inference becomes a larger fraction of total global compute needs, “total cost of ownership” (TCO) and “cost per token” have displaced raw pre-training performance as the dominant metrics for production-grade LLMs. This session will deconstruct the key hardware and software constraints that affect inference tokenomics. Join us to learn directly from the architect behind the world’s most complex compute buildout.

### Using Agentic AI to Transform Science: From Molecules to Materials
Speakers: Eun-Ah Kim (Cornell University), Marinka Zitnik (Harvard Medical School)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81745/
Type: Talk
AI is accelerating breakthroughs across scientific domains, transforming complex data into actionable insights for discovery and design. This session explores how agentic AI systems are being engineered to drive innovation—from decoding biological mechanisms for therapeutic science to modeling quantum matter for next-generation materials. Two visionary thought leaders will share how their research teams are applying robust, domain-specific AI frameworks to reshape the future of their fields.

### From Silicon to Solution: Building a World-Class Inference Engine for the AI Builder (Presented by GMI Cloud)
Speakers: Yujing Qian (GMI Cloud)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82083/
Type: Theater Talk
As NVIDIA Blackwell GPUs power production inference stacks originally built for previous generations, developers may observe unexpected bottlenecks. Higher peak compute and faster interconnects do not automatically translate to lower latency or better cost efficiency at scale. This session examines how inference system behavior changes when transitioning from NVIDIA Hopper to NVIDIA Blackwell GPUs. We’ll focus on concrete architectural shifts and show how the architecture improves. Rather than benchmarking peak performance, we’ll analyze observable patterns that emerge under real inference traffic and discuss how to redesign scheduling, batching, and monitoring strategies to fully benefit from NVIDIA Blackwell architecture.

### Why AI Platforms are Reclaiming Their GPU Infrastructure (Presented by Hydra Host)
Speakers: Aaron Ginn (Hydra Host)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82240/
Type: Theater Talk
As AI platforms scale from pilots to production, infrastructure decisions become strategic choices that affect cost, reliability, and long-term competitiveness. This session explores why an increasing number of AI platforms are moving away from fully centralized cloud models and toward owning dedicated GPU infrastructure on bare metal. We will discuss how AI platforms are using AI factory models to improve performance predictability, reduce unit costs, and monetize compute directly, while maintaining control over where and how their models run.

### AI Digital Twin of the Climate System
Speakers: Elizabeth Barnes (Boston University), Laure Zanna (New York University)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81763/
Type: Talk
Scientists use computer simulations to understand how different parts of the Earth’s system interact, or to explore how they respond to external forcing. These simulations have been carried out using numerical models (global climate models, or GCMs). Recently, modern machine-learning-based emulators have been used to reproduce the same dynamics using data. We will present the first generation of AI digital twin of the climate system, using data from state-of-the-art GCMs. These AI emulators can simulate the climate system 100x faster than typical GCMs with one GPU. We will discuss how we build, evaluate, and leverage these emulators to make discoveries about the climate system. For example, it offers strategies to improve simulations of extreme weather such as hurricanes, and forcing them with past ocean states reveals how and why hurricane counts have varied over the past 1,000 years.

### Accelerate Grid Modernization for AI Growth Using NVIDIA Omniverse
Speakers: Brenden Russell (Southern California Edison), Adnan Chaudhry (Siemens Energy), Dennis Loktionov (SoftServe)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81683/
Type: Talk
Building physically accurate digital twins for electrical grid infrastructure is becoming critical to be able simulate and predict outages during extreme weather events. We'll discuss Southern California Edison's journey of adopting Omniverse as a platform to bring all its ecosystem partners to build digital twins of the grid. Presenters will showcase how SoftServe built the solution, demonstrating Southern California Edison's vision, and in parallel helped Siemens Energy build scalable product integrating physics and AI-based models from substation.

### Accelerated Building Blocks for Next-Generation AI+HPC Workloads
Speakers: Heidi Poxon (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81792/

Embracing next-generation technology like AI-infused scientific workflows involves blending familiar building blocks with innovation, timeliness, and purpose. Whether it's models pre-trained on petabyte-scale data for common forecasting tasks, or real-time digital twins, developers need building blocks to enable their vision. In this talk, we walk through developer software used to enable AI and HPC innovations, including recent enhancements that maximize the use of NVIDIA hardware and improve the developer experience.

### Accelerating the Path to Production: The Evolution of Enterprise Storage to Deliver AI-Ready Data
Speakers: Jacob Liberman (NVIDIA), Jason Hardy (NVIDIA)

Roughly 70% of AI projects fail to transition to production due to challenges related to data compliance and security. To address this challenge, enterprise storage systems are evolving from data delivery to knowledge and intelligence delivery systems. Join us to learn how NVIDIA is collaborating with leading partners from the industry on architectures that deliver AI-ready data to accelerate the path from proof of concept to production.

page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82167/

The conversation explores the critical intersections of hardware innovation, systems scaling, and algorithmic advancement needed to propel AI into the 2026–2030 era of agentic systems, ultra-low-latency reasoning, and energy-efficient scaling.

### Autonomous Networks: The New Brain of Telco Operations
Speakers: Lilach Ilan (NVIDIA), Amol Phadke (Tech Mahindra), Tunc Yorulmaz (Accenture), Séainín McCoy (BT), Rajeev Koodli (SoftBank)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82016/

Automation is not the same as autonomy. Automated networks follow predefined rules; autonomous networks understand context, analyze data, reason, act, and learn continuously. Autonomous networks are the next frontier for telecoms, and progress is accelerating rapidly. This panel brings together leading telecom operators and solution providers to show how agentic AI and large telco models are transforming operations. Speakers will share how they unify data across operations and business support systems and network domains and deploy AI agents that self-configure, self-optimize, and self-heal networks while improving performance and customer experience. Hear real-world examples of capabilities already in production, the NVIDIA software and infrastructure behind them, and what the next 12 months of innovation look like as operators advance toward higher levels of autonomy.

Learn how to design high-performance agent architectures that can dynamically plan, retrieve, and act in real time while scaling seamlessly across GPUs.

Through robust data workflows and scalable deployment strategies, this approach has enabled faster, cleaner, and more reliable delivery of AI-driven insights. Discover how this foundation is now being expanded across broader CAE disciplines, transforming modeling efficiency, data integrity, and decision-making throughout the organization.

Speakers: William Ye (Aivres Systems)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82095/

With drastically increasing power and thermal density in new-generation AI chips and servers, AI data centers face enormous challenges in effectively and efficiently cooling their AI computing infrastructure at all levels—node, rack, pod, and data center. In this session, we will share Aivres energy-efficient liquid-cooled AI server products and solutions based on NVIDIA GPUs that fully meet the needs and technical challenges of new-generation AI data centers. Building on the primary liquid-cooling loop design, we extend the discussion to the secondary cooling loops, coolant distribution units, power supply, and liquid-cooled server rack designs. Finally, we will showcase our reference solutions and deployments for new-generation liquid-cooling AI data centers.

page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81696/

### Real-Time Science and Engineering With AI Physics and Kit-CAE
Speakers: Jeff Larkin (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81781/
Type: Talk
Physical engineering is moving from static reports and siloed workflows to real‑time, interactive insight. This session shows how AI physics meets simulation‑driven engineering by putting NVIDIA’s GPU‑accelerated libraries and NVIDIA Omniverse open libraries first. We’ll cover accessing common computer-aided engineering (CAE) formats via OpenUSD technologies in kit-cae; high‑fidelity field visualization with RTX and IndeX; Warp‑accelerated CAE/visualization algorithms; physics NeMo surrogates made from solver-generated ground truth; and application streaming for instant, hardware‑agnostic review and collaboration. We’ll highlight current industry use cases and advancements in the area of digital twins for science and engineering. You’ll leave today with an adoption path and a clear view of how AI physics and Omniverse libraries can accelerate your organization.

### Redefining Vector DB Management: Uniting Massive Scalability With GPU-Accelerated Indexing (Presented by KIOXIA America)
Speakers: Rory Bolt (KIOXIA America, Inc.)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82093/
Type: Theater Talk
As generative AI reshapes enterprise workloads, the rapid growth of vector databases introduces new challenges for scalability and performance. Kioxia Corp. addresses these challenges with AiSAQ, an innovative approximate nearest neighbor search (ANNS) algorithm that breaks traditional DRAM limitations by leveraging SSDs for search operations. Now, AiSAQ adds powerful GPU utilization to vector indexing, transforming a time-consuming bottleneck into a highly accelerated workflow. In this session, discover how AiSAQ’s combination of SSD scalability and NVIDIA cuVS dramatically boosts throughput for demanding generative AI applications such as retrieval-augmented generation and semantic search. Gain insights into the benefits of integrating SSDs and GPUs, enabling robust, cost-effective solutions to meet the demands of today's AI-driven workloads.

Speakers: Ali Haddad (XRlabs LTD), Ernst Jonscher (OLYMPUS Europa SE & Co. KG)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81749/

Join XRlabs and Olympus for a session exploring how technologies such as NVIDIA Isaac for Healthcare are shaping innovation in surgical environments. The discussion will include an overview of Olympus' 3D 4K Exoscope technology, as well as XRlabs' simulation and model training expertise, highlighting how AI-powered workflows can help bridge the gap between digital and physical environments. You'll see how simulation, digital twins, and robotics are being used to enable safer procedures, faster training, and smarter decision-making in the operating room. From modeling complex anatomy to validating robotic tasks in virtual environments before potential deployment in patient care, this session will explore emerging approaches that may influence the future of medical device development and precision surgery.

Speakers: Nicole Carignan (Darktrace), Omar Santos (Cisco Systems), Daniel Rohrer (NVIDIA), Heather Adkins (Google)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81494/

As organizations integrate AI into production systems, new and complex security challenges are emerging. In this panel discussion, we’ll explore where AI models are most vulnerable—data poisoning, evasion, and prompt-based exploitation—and how adversarial red teaming can help identify and mitigate these risks before attackers do. Learn how research–industry collaboration drives the development of scalable defenses and practical tools for securing machine learning pipelines. By the end of this session, you’ll have a high-level playbook for building resilient AI systems in an evolving threat landscape.

### Sovereign World Models: Scaling AI Memory, Data Gravity, and Governance (Presented by DDN)
Speakers: Shilpa Kolhatkar (NVIDIA), Victor Ghadban (DDN)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82096/
Type: Talk
World models demand long-horizon reasoning that rapidly outgrows GPU memory, while sovereign AI requires strict control over where data and models live. This session shows how a data intelligence architecture designed with native multi-tenancy, KV Cache, and hybrid cloud capabilities can extend the AI pipeline beyond the GPU to deliver ultra-low-latency, high-concurrency access patterns and policy-based governance to scale world models under sovereign constraints. You'll leave with practical design patterns for building NVIDIA-accelerated AI factories that maintain performance without sacrificing sovereignty or cost-per-token.

### The Augmented Workforce: Build and Deploy AI Agents for Enterprise XR
Speakers: Greg Barbone (NVIDIA), Purnendu Mukherjee (Convai), Bhaskar Banerjee (GridRaster), Roman Hasenbeck (Ramblr), Javier Figueras (AWS)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81621/

The next generation of extended reality (XR) devices is set to revolutionize the hands-on workforce. In this session, learn how Ramblr, Convai, AWS and GridRaster built intelligent AI agents that deliver real-time guidance, boosting worker productivity and safety.

### Under the Hood of Building and Scaling AI-Native Applications (Presented by Together AI)
Speakers: Alan Yiu (Decagon), Charles Zedlewski (Together AI)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82344/
Type: Talk
Building AI-native applications is fundamentally different from traditional software—and scaling them demands infrastructure designed for models, not just compute. Decagon builds AI agents for enterprise customer support across voice, chat, and email, backed by a research org that trains and fine-tunes specialized models to execute complex workflows through Agent Operating Procedures. As Decagon scaled, running autonomous agents reliably and economically became the defining challenge—especially for real-time voice, where silence reads as failure and latency is instantly perceptible. In this session, learn how Together AI, the AI-native cloud, helped Decagon turn a multi-model agent stack into a production system that meets sub-second latency targets and stays reliable through traffic surges.

### Vertically Integrated AI Cloud: Accelerating AI Development (Presented by IREN)
Speakers: Kent Draper (IREN)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82317/
Type: Talk
As AI workloads scale, infrastructure complexity increases — across power, cooling, networking, and compute. For customers deploying large GPU clusters, the question is no longer just about access to hardware, but about how reliably and predictably that infrastructure can be delivered and operated. This session examines the role of vertical integration in building AI cloud environments at scale. IREN develops its own sites, secures grid interconnections, engineers high-voltage substations, designs and constructs data centers, and deploys and operates its own GPU clusters — all within a single operating platform. By controlling the full stack — from power infrastructure through to compute — the platform reduces dependency on third parties, shortens deployment timelines, and provides tighter alignment between facility design and cluster architecture.

Speakers: Eiso Kant (Poolside)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82224/

We’ll walk through the evolving role of AI across the software life cycle—from ideation and system design to coding, testing, deployment, and continuous improvement.

### Building Sovereign AI: Scaling 100B+ Model Training on NVIDIA Blackwell Infrastructure (Presented by Lablup, Inc.)
Speakers: Jeongkyu Shin (Lablup, Inc.)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82047/
Type: Theater Talk
Dive deep into the real-world challenges of training 100 billion-plus parameter foundation models from scratch on NVIDIA Blackwell GPUs. We'll share hands-on experiences from a national-scale sovereign AI initiative, covering fault-tolerant orchestration, storage I/O optimization, and NVIDIA Collective Communication Library (NCCL) tuning strategies that keep massive training jobs running reliably across more than 500 B200 GPUs. At this scale, GPU errors, NCCL timeouts, and storage slowdowns begin to appear. Learn how we built a resilient stack with automated failure recovery, resolved 10x storage degradation from NFS driver issues, tackled MXFP8 precision stability on Blackwell, and tuned RoCE and NVIDIA InfiniBand networks through cross-vendor collaboration.

### Scaling the AI Factory: Eliminating Power and Thermal Barriers with Two-Phase Cooling (Presented by ZutaCore)
Speakers: My Truong (ZutaCore)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82049/
Type: Theater Talk
As AI infrastructure evolves toward AI factories and megawatt-scale deployments, thermals are emerging as a primary barrier to sustaining GPU performance. Rising chip power and continuous AI workloads are pushing traditional air and facility-water cooling beyond their practical limits, leading to throttling, energy inefficiency, and infrastructure constraints. In this session, My D. Truong, CTO of ZutaCore, will explain how waterless two-phase direct-to-chip cooling removes heat at the silicon through phase-change heat transfer, enabling stable GPU temperatures and sustained performance under dynamic AI workloads. The presentation will highlight two-phase cooling architectures designed for NVIDIA HGX™ B300 systems, introduce support for PCIe servers built with NVIDIA RTX PRO™ 6000 Blackwell Server Edition GPUs, and discuss a modular waterless data center architecture developed with Munters for scaling next-generation AI infrastructure.

### Ozaki Scheme: Addressing the Accuracy Challenge in the Era of Low-Precision Accelerators
Speakers: Katsuhisa Ozaki (Shibaura Institute of Technology)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82285/
Type: Talk
This talk explores the Ozaki Scheme, including its two variants, as a practical approach for recovering high accuracy from low-precision computations in numerical linear algebra. Participation is important as it sheds light on how we can effectively respond to the accuracy challenges posed by next-generation accelerator architectures.

Agile Robots is driving a shift in industrial automation—from systems that follow predefined rules to machines that perceive, learn, and act in the physical world through AI. This talk explores how Agile Robots builds on deep experience in industrial automation and combines it with advanced AI to realize physical AI as a new paradigm for designing work, manufacturing, and production, where intelligence is embodied in robotic systems, rather than confined to software. The presentation highlights how Agile Robots is developing today’s and the next generation's robotic systems—including humanoid robots—by integrating NVIDIA Isaac open frameworks and libraries to bring AI-driven flexibility, autonomy, and collaboration to the factory floor, and outlines the technological trajectory ahead.

### Al + Quantum: Unlocking the Next Era of Scientific and Industrial Breakthroughs (Presented by Quantinuum)
Speakers: Vishal Shrotriya (Quantinuum)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82178/
Type: Theater Talk
The convergence of AI and quantum computing is not a distant vision—it’s unfolding now. Generative Quantum AI (GenQAI) is at the forefront of this shift, creating a feedback loop where quantum computers generate novel data that can enhance classical AI models, while AI systems learn from and guide quantum exploration. Quantinuum has been using the NVIDIA CUDA-Q platform to bring these two technologies together. Their convergence unlocks more than technical achievements—it redefines what’s possible. In pharmaceuticals, it makes the acceleration of drug discovery possible by exploring chemical spaces classical computing cannot reach. In materials science, it makes breakthroughs in superconductors and batteries possible that can transform energy and mobility. In finance and logistics, it opens the door to solving optimization problems that define competitiveness.

### Maximize Tokens-per-Watt With AI Factory Digital Twins, From Chip to Grid (Presented by Siemens)
Speakers: Ales Alajbegovic (Siemens)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82069/

AI factories are becoming mission‑critical, gigawatt‑scale infrastructure that must be engineered as a single, connected system. This session presents a “chip‑to‑grid” workflow focused on two key performance indicators: time‑to‑token and watts‑per‑token. It begins at the silicon layer, where AI‑native, GPU‑accelerated electronic design automation that utilizes Fuse and NVIDIA Nemotron accelerates design cycles. The workflow then extends to the factory, where Siemens Digital Twin Composer with NVIDIA Omniverse enables decision‑grade digital twins across power, cooling, and layout before construction. The session will conclude with an operational reference architecture that improves efficiency, resilience, and uptime. Join us to explore how digital twins redefine AI factory design, construction, and operations—creating a new level of operational excellence.

### AI No Longer Benefits From High-Capacity SSDs—It Requires Them (Presented by Solidigm)
Speakers: Ace Stryker (Solidigm)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82305/

Huge training datasets are not new. But massive context windows, extended workflows, and rapid iteration are causing a data explosion on the inference side that analysts have dubbed the “memory apocalypse.” Demand vastly outpaces supply, putting AI users in a crunch. The solution: modern SSDs that are fast enough to relieve the pressure while also offering storage in the hundreds of terabytes, enabling unprecedented scale and efficiency.

### Discover Top In-Demand Learning Paths Across Europe, the Middle East, and Africa
Speakers: Juan Jose Durillo Barrionuevo (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81560/
Type: Talk
We invite you to join us as we explore NVIDIA's Deep Learning Institute Training Catalogue, uncovering practical use cases and real-world applications that extend beyond standard course descriptions. In this session, our NVIDIA experts will guide you through some of our most popular and in-demand learning paths, demonstrating how they can deepen your AI knowledge, strengthen your skills, and support your career development.

### Engineering Medical-Grade Platforms for Real-Time AI
Speakers: Pietro Salvagnini (Cosmo MedTech AI), Nhan Ngo Dinh (Cosmo MedTech AI)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82258/
Type: Talk
Join us to learn how to turn NVIDIA IGX and Holoscan into a reusable, medical-grade platform for real-time AI. We'll walk through the complete stack, from system architecture and safety concepts to Holoscan graph design and containerized SaMD deployment, with a focus on the decisions, trade-offs, and patterns you can apply to your own devices. The session emphasizes how to meet strict latency and reliability constraints in a pass-through video pipeline, how to design a common regulated platform that can host multiple AI applications, and how to enable external teams to build and ship models on top of that platform.

### Elevating Business Jet Customer Experience With an Enhanced Rendering Workflow
Speakers: Axel Cocat (Dassault Aviation), Jean-Charles Fedini (Dassault Aviation)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81985/
Type: Talk
We'll walk through how we built our graphics pipeline in Omniverse, an upgrade that slashed rendering times by nearly a factor of 100 and cut our production cycles in half. This improvement now lets us deliver photorealistic videos alongside traditional renderings, significantly enhancing what we can offer our customers. We’ll delve into our customer workflow and the custom tooling we’ve developed around Omniverse to maximize efficiency.

### Spain's First Physical AI Lab: Empowering the Northern Spanish Industrial Ecosystem
Speakers: Jorge Posada (Vicomtech), Jairo Sánchez (Vicomtech)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81988/

We will share our experience in building the first physical AI research lab in northern Spain, dedicated to empowering the local industrial ecosystem. This lab serves as a collaborative space where researchers can design, prototype, and validate cutting-edge technologies in robotics and automation. By providing access to advanced physical AI tools such as NVIDIA Omniverse and Isaac platforms, we enable rapid development and testing before deployment in real-world scenarios. Local industries can actively engage with these innovations, gaining hands-on experience and exploring practical applications that drive competitiveness and technological adoption. We'll show how collaboration between academic research and industrial implementation can foster a dynamic environment for innovation and growth.

Speakers: Ava Arnaz (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81552/

Explore the NVIDIA resiliency tools that keep AI systems reliable at scale. Learn how to match these solutions to your operational challenges to build a practical adoption roadmap.

page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81720/

page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82156/

NVIDIA’s developer training programs support faculty teaching students and researchers through downloadable teaching kits, a cloud-based GPU-accelerated platform, and technical certification exams. Instructional content spans foundational topics—deep learning, accelerated computing, and data science—and advanced areas including agentic AI, natural language processing, and generative AI. Online courses and instructor-led workshops with project-based assessments help faculty align coursework with certifications and support students in earning recognized qualifications. We highlight ECPI integrating GPU-accelerated AI into student projects, and Lebanese American University using hands-on, faculty-led workshops to advance applied learning and prepare students for real-world AI applications and industry pathways.

### Agentic AI 101
Speakers: Erik Pounds (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82432/
Type: Talk
Agentic AI is evolving rapidly, shifting AI systems from passive responders to active systems that can plan, reason, and take action. In this session, we break down what agentic AI is, how the space has changed in the last year, and how both frontier models and open models are enabling a new class of AI applications. You will learn the core components of agentic systems, where different model types fit, and practical ways to get started building agents today. This session is designed to give developers and technical leaders a clear mental model for agentic AI and a concrete path from experimentation to real applications.

### An Introduction to Robot Simulation
Speakers: Rishabh Chadha (NVIDIA), Asawaree Bhide (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81488/
Type: Tutorial
Physical AI is transforming how robots interact with the world. Building these advanced systems requires three computers: compute for training, advanced simulation for validation, and efficient edge deployment. With a simulation-first approach, robots can train across millions of scenarios, generalize faster, and adapt to real-world complexity. In this technical deep-dive session, learn core robotics simulation workflows, from building virtual environments to configuring robots, sensors, and physics, using NVIDIA Isaac Sim and Isaac Lab, built with NVIDIA Omniverse libraries. You'll also explore how to generate and leverage synthetic data with NVIDIA Cosmos world foundation models to train and validate robot perception and control policies before deploying them in the real world.

### Best Practices for Hashmap Optimization on NVIDIA Grace and Vera CPUs
Speakers: Yuzhong Wen (NVIDIA), Matthias Langer (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81658/
Type: Talk
Efficient hashmaps are among the most critical data structures in modern data analytics, genomics, and recommender systems. In this talk, we'll deep-dive into the amazing world of hashmaps for CPUs. We will discuss types, approaches, and properties of different CPU hashmap implementations with respect to our current data-center CPU platform NVIDIA Grace and the upcoming NVIDIA Vera platform. We'll tackle practical considerations for optimizing hashmap construction and lookups on NVIDIA CPUs, share lessons learned from integrating them into data analytical workloads, and describe theoretical performance limits, tuning knobs, and less well-known techniques to improve hashmap performance.

### Building America’s AI-Native 6G: Architecture, Timelines, and Ecosystem Innovations
Speakers: Soma Velayutham (NVIDIA), Ankur Kapoor (T-Mobile), Thomas W. Rondeau (US Department of War), Bill Vass (Booz Allen)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82023/
Type: Talk
AI‑native 6G is a strategic opportunity for the United States to lead the next era of wireless infrastructure, not just adopt it. Building on the AI-WIN initiative and the first all‑American AI‑native wireless stack announced in 2025, this session focuses on moving from concepts and demos to deployable systems. Speakers from government, industry, and the broader ecosystem will explain why AI‑native 6G matters for U.S. competitiveness and security, how policy and public‑private partnerships accelerate progress, and why an implementation‑first approach must advance with standards. The session will cover deployment timelines, key milestones, progress on AI-RAN for 5G and 6G using the NVIDIA AI Aerial platform, and early lessons from AI‑native stack trials, including pre‑6G applications and innovations toward commercial‑grade AI‑native 6G networks.

### Data Analytics and Streaming Media
Speakers: Bianca Pryor (NVIDIA), Jason Harvey (BET+), Joao Sobreira (Advolve), Raghav Gupta (Relo Metrics)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82148/
