In this "Lightning Talks" session, industry experts will explore the evolving world of data analytics and streaming, covering AI-driven innovations in sponsorship analytics, data processing needs of streaming services, and real-time audience insights. Speakers will discuss how advanced data tools are driving end-to-end content automation and improving attribution accuracy, helping businesses optimize strategies and increase engagement. Gain actionable insights on the role of AI in refining analytics and enhancing operational efficiency in the streaming industry.

### Deploy Lambda’s Bare Metal Instances with NVIDIA Vera Rubin NVL72 & GB300 NVL72 (Presented by Lambda)
Speakers: Maxx Garrison (Lambda), Doug Pan (Lambda)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82151/
Type: Talk
We explore how Lambda’s bare-metal cloud unlocks the full performance of NVIDIA rack-scale systems by removing overhead and delivering the hardware as designed. You’ll learn how direct access to GPUs, CPUs, NVIDIA NVLink, and high-bandwidth fabrics increases efficiency, reduces time-to-train, and enables scaling for frontier AI workloads.

### Magic Attention: A Composable Framework for Exploring Warp-Specialized Attention on Blackwell
Speakers: Manish Gupta (Magic AI, Inc.)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82294/
Type: Talk
Optimizing attention on NVIDIA Blackwell demands more than a monolithic kernel to handle diverse models, training, inference, and RL workloads. We introduce Magic Attention, a high-performance, composable framework built on CUTLASS/CuTe that navigates the vast scheduling design space of the FlashAttention algorithm on the NVIDIA Blackwell architecture. We discuss four different forward attention kernel schedules, leveraging two Tensor Memory (TMEM) partitionings for deep pipeline overlap, and convolution-inspired runtime tensor packing. We will also discuss the use of softmax emulation and correction skipping.

### Maximize Memory Bandwidth on Modern GPUs: Practical Techniques, Patterns, and Worked Examples
Speakers: Matthew Martineau (NVIDIA), Benedikt Dorschner (NVIDIA), Sam Mish (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81666/
Type: Tutorial
Modern GPU generations (since Volta) have increased memory bandwidth faster than streaming multiprocessor (SM) count, which makes the classic “add more threads” recipe insufficient for many memory-bound kernels. This tutorial gives hands-on, implementation-focused guidance to saturate memory bandwidth on contemporary NVIDIA GPUs using loop unrolling, vectorization, asynchronous pipelining (Tensor memory accelerator, or TMA), cache prefetching, and other techniques. Through step-by-step worked examples (e.g., stencils, SpMV), we show common pitfalls (peeling, misalignment, tiny kernels) and practical fixes, plus variants of TMA usage and a producer-consumer TMA pattern you can reuse.

### Next-Generation Intelligent Surgical Robots
Speakers: Mahdi Azizian (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81742/
Type: Talk
Explore how NVIDIA technologies are transforming the design and scalability of intelligent surgical robots, enabling software-defined, AI-powered systems that improve outcomes across the healthcare ecosystem. Learn how NVIDIA Holoscan and agentic frameworks are shaping the future of smart operating rooms and hospitals.

### Powering Agentic AI in Automotive: From Enterprise and Manufacturing Operations to In-Vehicle Intelligence
Speakers: Sri Subramanian (NVIDIA), Manoj Chathankulangara Rajan (Tata Consultancy Services), Jordan Tauriainen (ServiceNow), Tao Ye (Lyft), Harsha Padmanabha (Visteon), Felix Friedmann (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82406/
Type: Panel
Automotive AI is rapidly evolving from isolated pilots into end-to-end, production-scale deployments—spanning manufacturing operations, enterprise workflows, and in-vehicle intelligence. In this session, industry leaders from ServiceNow, Visteon, Lyft, and Tata Consultancy Services share how NVIDIA Nemotron models are being operationalized across the automotive value chain to deliver measurable business, operational, and engineering impact.

### Python All the Way Down: Speed-of-Light CUDA Without Leaving Python
Speakers: Ashwin Srinath (NVIDIA), Ianna Osborne (Princeton University)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81531/
Type: Talk
How would you write a Python library that uses CUDA today? Most Python GPU libraries—PyTorch, CuPy, RAPIDS, and others—still build on CUDA C++ because many core CUDA building blocks have only ever existed in C++. That’s now changing. New Python-native CUDA libraries bring formerly C++-only capabilities directly into Python, enabling high-performance GPU functionality without bindings or C++ backends. This talk shows how to build modern GPU libraries with these tools, and what becomes possible when it’s Python all the way down. To provide a real-world example of using these new tools to build accelerated Python libraries, we will present a case study of how the Awkward Array Python library used for high energy physics at CERN is replacing its custom CUDA C++ kernels with cuda.compute, delivering tremendous speedups while making the codebase vastly easier to evolve and maintain.

### Quick Start to Accelerated Quantum Supercomputing
Speakers: Danny Bulmash (IQM Quantum Computers), Monica Van Dieren (NVIDIA), Mark Wolf (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81553/
Type: Tutorial
Get a hands-on introduction to NVIDIA CUDA-Q, the platform for seamlessly orchestrating both quantum and classical processors alongside each other in accelerated quantum supercomputers. Whether you are new to the field or an experienced developer, this session walks you through building a hybrid application to demonstrate the execution of quantum-classical workflows across NVIDIA GPUs and IQM’s quantum hardware.

Speakers: Frances Waldmann (Gibson Dunn), Jessica Butler (NVIDIA), Sean Litton (Tech Coalition), Kerry Smith (Internet Watch Foundation)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81551/

Ensuring the safety of children and young people is a global effort. This panel brings together leaders from industry, law, and civil society engaged in protecting children and teens online to discuss what tools and techniques are available for companies interested in joining the fight.

### The AI-Native Digital Health Stack: A Developer's Guide to 2026
Speakers: Brad Genereaux (NVIDIA), Raghav Mani (NVIDIA), Adi Margolin (NVIDIA), Arun Venkatesan (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81659/

The digital health stack is undergoing a fundamental shift, moving from siloed data and manual processes to an AI-native, interoperable future. For developers, the components of 2026's most effective systems—powered by generative AI reasoning and advanced speech models—are already here. Join this digital health developer day event to get the essential know-how to reimagine your entire stack. We'll provide the overviews, strategies, and case studies you need to build what's next in healthcare.

### The Ecosystem Engine: Co-Creating the AI Factory (Presented by Vertiv)
Speakers: Martin Olsen (Vertiv)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82232/
Type: Talk
This session explores how deep, pre-validated collaboration is the only path to de-risking the AI factory. We will discuss how digital twin-driven design allows the ecosystem to visualize and solve thermal and power challenges before physical deployment, enabling a "design once, deploy everywhere" approach. We will examine how this co-creation strategy supports the data center as a unit of compute, ensuring that power, thermal, and IT systems operate as a single, synchronized organism, rather than disjointed components. Anchored in the Vertiv Frontiers 2026 trends, we’ll show how ecosystem-driven integration becomes mission-critical as architectures evolve. We’ll also cover how digital twins—using physically accurate simulation—enable teams to validate designs, failure scenarios, and performance before steel is cut, reducing schedule and operational risk.

### Turn Your Data Estate Into AI Fuel (Presented by NetApp)
Speakers: Tore Sundelin (NetApp)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82103/
Type: Talk
Enterprises sit on massive data estates, often 100 exabytes or more of unstructured information. Yet 95% of AI pilots fail to reach production. The problem isn't a lack of data. It's that the data aren't AI-ready. In this session, discover how NetApp and NVIDIA are transforming enterprise data infrastructure into an AI-ready data platform that fuels AI factories. Learn how NetApp AI Data Engine, designed on NVIDIA AI Data Platform, refines raw enterprise data into AI-ready intelligence. See how NetApp AFX and validated AIPod configurations deliver the performance foundation your AI factory demands. The result is agentic AI that can truly understand and unlock the value hidden across your business.

Speakers: Wenchen Li (AInvest), Adam Wang (Ainvest), Damien Zhang (HiThink)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81729/

This talk presents a DeepResearch-powered long-context framework for financial multi-modal conversational models, two specialized financial benchmarks (BizFinBench and MME-Finance), the any-to-any omni architecture for real-time cross-modal agentic interactions, and a general evaluation engine for standardized AI performance evaluation across key capabilities.

### AI Literacy at Scale: K-to-Career Access That Delivers Real Student Outcomes
Speakers: Paul Turnbull (Mid-Pacific Institute), Ryan Trattner (StudyFetch), Muffie Waterman (NVIDIA), Kollin Napier (Mississippi Artificial Intelligence Network (MAIN))
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82166/

AI literacy has moved from a "nice-to-have" to a readiness requirement. The open question is execution: how schools build access that scales, supports educators, and reaches students across different learning paths, geographies, and resources. This panel brings together education leaders and practitioners shaping that execution from four angles: classroom and school implementation, learning science and workforce-relevant skill-building, platform-level delivery at scale, and institutional systems that connect education to long-term opportunity. Together, the panel will share practical approaches to expand AI literacy and access without turning schools into pilot sites. You'll leave with a clear view of what to teach, how to equip educators, how to deploy tools responsibly, and how to build pathways that connect early literacy to real-world readiness over time.

### AI-Powered Huge Ensembles for Portfolio Climate Risk Management
Speakers: Duncan Anderson (S&P Global Energy), Georg Ertl (NVIDIA), Danielle Montagne (S&P Global Energy)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82302/
Type: Talk
Discover how large‑scale AI‑accelerated ensemble forecasting is reshaping the way we assess physical climate risk in financial portfolios. This session dives into practical, data‑driven methods for quantifying asset‑level climate exposure—bridging advanced climate modeling with real‑world financial and energy use cases. You’ll learn how massive ensembles help uncover cross‑dependencies among temperature, wind, and solar patterns that drive volatility in energy commodities, grid stability, and portfolio performance under extreme conditions.

### Advancing Autonomous Vehicles With World Models
Speakers: Sanja Fidler (NVIDIA and University of Toronto)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82446/
Type: Talk
Simulation remains a cornerstone for accelerating robotics and autonomous vehicle (AV) development, enabling policies to be evaluated ahead of real-world deployment and drastically reducing development time. Achieving meaningful results requires simulators that capture both the environment and the world’s response to AI agents with high fidelity, across diverse and challenging scenarios. NVIDIA Cosmos has been specialized for autonomous vehicles, incorporating novel architecture changes and optimizations to deliver real-time simulation for the first time. In this talk, we explore the breakthrough that powers closed-loop evaluation of AV policies, empowering developers to iterate faster, test more rigorously, and bring safer, more robust autonomous systems to the real world.

### Architect for AI Breakthroughs: Co-Design Memory + Storage for What’s Now and Next (Presented by Samsung Semiconductor)
Speakers: Eyal Pnini (Samsung Semiconductor), Pankaj Kalra (Samsung Semiconductor, Inc.)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82076/
Type: Talk
As AI workloads evolve toward complex agentic reasoning and large-scale physical intelligence, the integration between compute and the memory-storage hierarchy has become vital. This session explores collaborative architectural approaches co-designed to support the transition to the NVIDIA Rubin platform powering AI factories, alongside platforms powering AI at the edge and physical AI. We will discuss the technical integration of HBM4, HBM4E, and SOCAMM2 within accelerated CPU-GPU clusters to optimize system-level throughput and thermal efficiency, while also examining the role of high-performance PCIe Gen6 storage in emerging inference context management frameworks. Join us to explore how these synchronized memory and storage layers provide the robust performance and reliability required for the current state and next frontier of AI.

### Best Practices in Building and Packaging CUDA Python Libraries
Speakers: Jonathan Dekhtiar (NVIDIA), Vyas Ramasubramani (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82370/
Type: Talk
Packaging CUDA‑enabled Python code can seem daunting. With many build backends, Python wheels, conda packages, and CUDA compatibility rules in the mix, even experienced developers hesitate. The key is separating Python packaging complexity from CUDA‑specific challenges. This session starts with a deep dive in build backends and packaging formats like wheels and conda packages, then moves into compiled extensions and how tools like pip, uv, and conda ensure broad compatibility. We’ll explore CUDA‑accelerated packages from pure‑Python wrappers to custom CUDA C/C++ kernels, emphasizing practical decisions and best practices for reliable installs.

### Build an AI-Ready Data Center: Practical Insights From YTL, Wiwynn, and NVIDIA (Presented by Wiwynn Corp.)
Speakers: Ted Pang (Wiwynn Corp.)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82034/
Type: Talk
This session reveals how YTL partnered with Wiwynn and NVIDIA to build an AI-ready data center. We’ll walk through the NVIS deployment methodology—covering site qualification, high-density GPU rack integration, and power/cooling validation under real-world stress. You’ll also learn how to implement end-to-end observability and manage liquid-cooling risks. Finally, we share benchmarking results using MLPerf and AI workloads to confirm the environment meets AI-ready standards.

### Building the Architecture of Advertising Intelligence: How GPU-Accelerated AI Agents are Reshaping Digital Advertising
Speakers: Rajeev Goel (PubMatic), Vasu Cherlopalle (PubMatic)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81782/
Type: Talk
Digital advertising now rivals high-frequency trading in both speed and complexity: tens of millions of auctions occur every second, each demanding a decision in under a millisecond. AI is transforming the industry as agents autonomously plan, negotiate, and execute media transactions in real time. This session details how PubMatic re-engineered its global infrastructure using NVIDIA-accelerated computing to power some of the world’s first agentic advertising transactions, offering a blueprint for how GPU-driven intelligence is reshaping not just programmatic advertising, but real-time digital markets of every kind.

### From Benchmark Silos to an Interoperable AI Evaluation Ecosystem
Speakers: Alexandre Lacoste (ServiceNow AI Research)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82445/
Type: Talk
As agent benchmarks multiply, the AI research community is paying a growing integration tax, with each new benchmark requiring custom infrastructure and tooling. This session introduces Common Unified Benchmark Environments (CUBE), a universal benchmarking protocol built on MCP and Gym that defines shared APIs for tasks, benchmarks, packages, and registries. We explore CUBE’s design and show how it integrates with existing evaluation workflows, including early reference integrations validated in collaboration with NVIDIA using NeMo Gym and NeMo Evaluator. Learn how to adopt CUBE, integrate it into your own tooling, and contribute to a more interoperable and scalable benchmarking ecosystem.

### From Isolation to Integration: Evolving Confidential Computing for a Scalable, Secure Future
Speakers: Emily Sakata (NVIDIA), Nelly Porter (Google)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81638/
Type: Talk
Generative AI is rapidly becoming the defining workload of modern computing, but securing these powerful systems without compromising performance is still a challenge for most enterprises. This session dives into how you can lock down your most valuable AI assets—models, data, and prompts—while continuing to push the limits on speed, scale and insights. Google and NVIDIA will share how confidential computing on NVIDIA GPUs can protect your data and model using accelerated computing, so your information stays shielded even while in use.

### Global Governance and the Future of AI Safety Regulation
Speakers: Iain Cunningham (NVIDIA), Jennifer Barrera (California Chamber of Commerce), Minesh Tanna (Simmons & Simmons LLP)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81548/
Type: Panel
The pace of AI regulations is increasing, and the complexity of complying with different laws is challenging for global companies. For example, EU member countries are passing "companion" regulations for the Artificial Intelligence Act. Meanwhile, the U.S. federal government hasn't adopted any AI regs, but many states have, and the current administration's focus differs from that of the previous administration. At the same time, Congress is focused primarily on child safety. How can companies comply with laws in such a fragmented environment?

### Healthcare Reimagined: Bridging Digital Intelligence and Physical Autonomy
Speakers: Prerna Dogra (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81599/

Robotics and AI are redefining healthcare, accelerating diagnostics, surgery, patient care, and operational efficiency. We'll highlight how NVIDIA technologies drive this transformation, from physical AI systems that perform precision tasks and address workforce shortages to digital AI agents that interpret multimodal data, predict patient needs, and guide decision-making. We'll dive into the latest NVIDIA technology—spanning cutting-edge hardware, AI factories, and next-gen simulation—enabling both physical and digital robots. You’ll see how these technologies unify sensing, perception, planning, control, and generative reasoning into deployable devices. You'll also learn how medical technology developers are building on NVIDIA’s ecosystem of tools, reference workflows, and partner solutions to accelerate R&D, improve system intelligence, and bring autonomous capabilities to life.

### How HBM4 Unlocks Efficient LLM Serving at Scale (Presented by SK hynix)
Speakers: Donguk Moon (SK hynix)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81977/
Type: Talk
Explore how next-generation high-bandwidth memory (HBM4) improves large language model serving by addressing two critical inference constraints: memory bandwidth and capacity. Through detailed profiling and controlled scaling studies across modern LLMs and reasoning- and agent-based workloads, we show how HBM4’s substantially higher bandwidth and increased capacity translate into lower latency and higher throughput in LLM serving systems, and we project the performance gains production AI systems can expect from GPU platforms equipped with HBM4.

### Master AI Infrastructure At Scale: A DevOps Playbook
Speakers: Kristof De Brouwer (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81422/

Your AI factory is ready for action—now it’s time to put it to work. In this session, we will walk you through the essential steps for deploying and managing your AI infrastructure. Aiming at teams beginning their AI factory journey, we'll focus on real-world deployment practices and insights drawn from leading customer environments. We’ll discuss how to streamline cluster onboarding, configuration, and monitoring, laying the foundation for efficient and resilient operations from Day One.

### Performance Without Compromise: How Memory Innovation is Central to AI System Effectiveness (Presented by Micron)
Speakers: Girish Cherussery (Micron Technology)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82079/
Type: Talk
Artificial intelligence is driving unprecedented computational demands that rely on massive data access at low latency. Learn how advanced semiconductor technologies—high-bandwidth memory, low-power DRAM, and next-generation storage—have become critical to meeting these computing challenges in AI systems. We will spotlight how Micron’s innovations reduce power consumption in large-scale AI training and inference systems while delivering the bandwidth to advance next-generation generative AI.

### Transform Content Delivery With Generative AI at the Edge
Speakers: Kevin Deierling (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81690/
Type: Talk
As AI reshapes digital experiences, content delivery needs a major transformation. Traditional networks built for static caching can’t meet real-time, personalized expectations. Modern applications require generative AI inference at the edge, close to users and data, to meet strict performance, privacy, and efficiency needs. Learn how edge AI redefines how content is created, delivered, and monetized. By combining generative, agentic, and future physical AI with NVIDIA accelerated computing and software, you can synthesize content in real time and tailor experiences to users and markets. Learn how advanced edge compute and intelligent data systems reduce data movement, maintain sovereignty, and deliver adaptive, low-latency experiences across augmented/virtual reality, internet of things, and autonomous systems. Join us to see how Gen AI at the edge unlocks new value and innovation.

### Wireless Network Digital Twins: Use Cases for 5G and 6G
Speakers: Satoshi Konishi (KDDI Research), CC Chong (NVIDIA), Samir Kumar (Nokia)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82022/
Type: Talk
Wireless network digital twins are a critical building block for AI‑native networks, especially as next‑generation 6G systems will be born first in simulation. NVIDIA Aerial Omniverse Digital Twin (AODT) creates realistic, physics‑based replicas of wireless networks that reflect radio conditions, topology, and traffic patterns, allowing operators and partners to model, test, and optimize 5G and 6G safely before touching the live network. In this session, NVIDIA and partners will show how AODT supports workflows from network planning and optimization to validating new AI‑for‑RAN algorithms and agentic AI scenarios, where agents explore actions in the twin first. Speakers will share real use cases and how integrating AODT helps them create and run more intelligent networks today, and why digital twins are becoming the default way to build AI‑native wireless networks of the future.

### AI Performance and Inference Economics for Engineers
Speakers: Eduardo Alvarez (NVIDIA), Farshad Ghodsian (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81996/
Type: Talk
This technical deep dive analyzes the cost drivers for AI systems, focusing on the interdependent relationship between total cost of ownership (TCO) and performance. We will show how the influence of extreme co-design, spanning silicon, software, and the rack-scale system, unlocks new efficiency levers. The session will detail how these integrated optimizations fundamentally improve operational metrics like cost per token and token per watt in modern deployments.

### Accelerate GPU Scientific Computing With nvmath-python
Speakers: Sergey Maydanov (NVIDIA), Aart Bik (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81581/
Type: Talk
In 2024, NVIDIA unveiled nvmath-python, a library designed to bridge the gap between Python scientific community and NVIDIA CUDA-X math libraries, and it’s now generally available. You’ll learn what makes nvmath-python a useful addition for GPU-accelerated computing with Python, along with how to use the library’s unique capabilities to reach extreme levels of performance to solve cutting-edge scientific problems previously unimaginable in Python.

### An Introduction to the Newton Physics Engine for Robotics
Speakers: Miles Macklin (NVIDIA), Moritz Baecher (Disney Research Imagineering), Erik Frey (Google DeepMind), Deepak Pathak (Skild AI)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81613/
Type: Tutorial
Robots learn faster and safer in simulation, but complex joints, balance, and contact-rich motions challenge current physics engines. Developers need high-fidelity simulation so virtual training transfers safely to real robots. This session with NVIDIA, Google DeepMind, Skild AI, and Disney Research introduces Newton, an open-source, GPU-accelerated physics engine built on NVIDIA Warp and OpenUSD to advance robot learning. Newton’s flexible design and multiple solvers enable realistic simulations—like walking through snow, or handling cups and fruit—and reliable deployment in the physical world. Explore Newton’s architecture, integration into robot-learning pipelines, and ways to develop new solvers for safer, more capable robots across diverse environments and tasks.

### Building Sovereign AI Systems Under Real-World Constraints (Presented by Radiant)
Speakers: Christopher Galloway (Radiant)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82367/
Type: Talk
This session examines sovereign AI as a systems engineering challenge that emerges from the interaction of architecture, control planes, operations, and life-cycle ownership. Drawing on real-world experience designing large-scale AI infrastructure under persistent supply and operational constraints, the talk focuses on the long-term consequences of foundational design decisions and how they shape autonomy, resilience, and adaptability over the life of an AI system.

### Climate Prediction and Smooth Navigation Use Cases
Speakers: Tapio Schneider (California Institute of Technology), Wei-Chang Yeh (National Tsing Hua University)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81566/
Type: Talk
This session will introduce two use cases of NVIDIA platform adoption—one in climate prediction, and the other in smooth navigation. Climate Prediction: Accurate climate prediction relies on capturing small-scale processes, such as clouds and turbulence, which remain elusive in traditional models. This talk presents the CliMA solution: a physics-AI hybrid Earth System Model built in Julia to run natively on NVIDIA GPUs. Leveraging GPU acceleration, researchers generate large ensembles to drive a "calibrate, emulate, sample" workflow. Generalized Navigation: the CUDA-accelerated Delta-Corridor framework utilizes Delta-Corridor spatial modeling to achieve reliable and smooth generalized navigation with Isaac and Cosmos. By applying continuous safety corridors, it ensures reliable navigation for mobile robots, boats, and autonomous vehicles in dynamic environments.

### Close the Sim-to-Real Gap: Next-Gen Techniques for Synthetic Data Mastery (Presented by World Wide Technology)
Speakers: Paige Reiter (World Wide Technology)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82220/
Type: Talk
Stop relying on limited real-world datasets. Join us to explore proven techniques to leverage NVIDIA Omniverse Replicator and NVIDIA Cosmos to create synthetic imagery for building robust computer vision models that recognize rare objects and challenging conditions. We will dive deep into synthetic imagery best practices, the NVIDIA hardware and software stacks that accelerate the process, and the critical decision points that maximize your synthetic data return on investment and accelerate your path to production-ready physical AI.

### Decoding Quantum Errors With AI
Speakers: Monica Van Dieren (NVIDIA), Mark Wolf (NVIDIA), Justin Lietz (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81868/
Type: Tutorial
Learn the basics of quantum error correction (QEC) and why decoding is a primary bottleneck for quantum computing. Using hands-on exercises, learn about key decoder metrics like accuracy, scalability, throughput, and latency and their implications for quantum computation. Lastly, implement your own AI decoder workflow using the CUDA-Q QEC library. We assume that you're familiar with the fundamentals of quantum computing (gates, circuits, measurement, etc.), but do not necessarily have experience with QEC.

### Enhance Data Center Efficiency and Throughput by Co-Scheduling on NVIDIA CPU+GPU Systems
Speakers: Brian Austin (NERSC), Mathias Wagner (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81853/
Type: Talk
Coherent NVIDIA CPU-GPU systems provide a versatile platform to support a wide spectrum of workloads. However not all jobs require all capabilities at scale. Co-scheduling CPU-only, partially accelerated, and fully GPU-accelerated jobs on NVIDIA CPU-GPU nodes can boost throughput and energy efficiency, removing the need for multiple dedicated clusters. We discuss isolation mechanisms including CPU affinity via cgroups, cache and memory bandwidth partitioning through ARM’s MPAM extensions, and InfiniBand QoS for network traffic separation. Learn to identify co-schedule candidates and assess scheduling policy design options and incentives for user adoption. We'll present workload studies aligned with NERSC’s upcoming Doudna system, demonstrating projected improvements in utilization and efficiency while highlighting open challenges in enabling fine-grained resource sharing.

### High Performance AI-RAN Made Simple; Python to CUDA Signal Processing at the Speed of Light
Speakers: Joao Morais (NVIDIA), Kobi Cohen-Arazi (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82158/
Type: Tutorial
The Aerial Framework has been designed from the ground up to meet the needs of 3GPP radio access networks (RANs)—signal processing workloads with microsecond latency requirements. It is a platform that unites research, test beds, and production deployments to solve development challenges for real-time applications. The session will give a hands-on tutorial to design algorithms in JAX/Torch and instantly deploy to real-time GPU pipelines with inline networking. GitHub: https://github.com/NVIDIA/aerial-framework

### Large-Scale JAX Training With Pipeline Parallelism
Speakers: Vinod Grover (NVIDIA), Nikolas Gritsch (Cohere), Anxhelo Xhebraj (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82141/
Type: Talk
We'll give an overview of the first practical system for enabling pipeline parallelism (PP) for JAX on NVIDIA GPUs. JaxPP accepts simple annotations on the training step functions, and then will introduce Multiple Programs operating on Multiple Data (MPMD). This system is a compiler that works on top of JAX and its XLA compiler. We will give an introduction to pipeline parallelism and how it works on training. We'll also describe our experience of using JaxPP effectively on Cohere's training models.

### Optimize Biologics Design Workflow With Auto-Tuned Models and Multi-Agent Systems
Speakers: Hu Ge (Wecomput Technology), Jiabo Li (Wecomput Technology)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81559/
Type: Talk
Applying AI to antibody therapeutics often presents significant engineering challenges—particularly in effectively linking structure prediction, molecular generation, and property optimization into a cohesive pipeline. Learn how breakthrough AI frameworks can be leveraged to architect a zero-code antibody design and optimization workflow. We'll demonstrate how multi-agent capabilities enable intelligent interpretation of complex R&D requirements—from de novo generation to multi-objective optimization—without the need for manual coding. We'll also share key learnings and identified bottlenecks while addressing the industry-wide “ADA prediction gap” through the construction of a high-fidelity immunogenicity simulation framework that models human immune responses, including MHC binding, APC presentation, and T-cell activation.

### Optimize Mixture-Of-Experts for Low-Latency Inference Decode on Blackwell
Speakers: Allard Hendriksen (NVIDIA), Petrick Liu (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81518/
Type: Tutorial
This ninja-level tutorial will guide you through the techniques to build a low-latency inference kernel for Blackwell using CUDA and PTX. You will learn about techniques like programmatic dependent launch, prologue minimization, lightweight CGA parallelism, and operand swapping in the Blackwell Tensor Cores.

### Optimizing Speech-To-Text Pipelines for Real-World Voice Agents
Speakers: Max Lu (Decagon)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82429/
Type: Talk
Every voice AI agent starts with the same problem: turning raw audio into something useful. This session shares what we've learned at Decagon building a production speech-to-text pipeline for customer service voice agents. We'll walk through the core components, including voice activity detection, turn detection, and transcription, and the failure modes we hit when deploying on real phone calls. Along the way, we'll cover the statistical and learned approaches we developed to handle these challenges, and how we think about evaluating ASR in a way that actually reflects agent performance.

### Physical AI for Healthcare Robotics—Part 1: Simulation-First Design and Accelerated Development
Speakers: Mostafa Toloui (NVIDIA), Filippo Filicori (Northwell Health), Yossi Bar (LEM Surgical), Axel Krieger (Johns Hopkins), David Noonan (Moon Surgical)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81821/
Type: Lightning Talk
Join Part 1 of a two-part curated session on how simulation-first design is changing the way healthcare robots are built. You’ll see how teams are using NVIDIA Physical AI—spanning world foundation models, physics-based simulation, synthetic data, and real-time sensor-to-action deployment—to move faster with less risk. This session focuses on how simulation and digital twins are being used in practice to speed up development, test decisions earlier, and optimize systems long before they reach the operating room

### Scaling Trustworthy Multi-Modal Video Intelligence: A Life Cycle-Aware Approach for Domain-Specific Real-World Platforms
Speakers: Jaeho Oh (PYLER)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81617/
Type: Talk
This session will demonstrate how to move beyond basic spatial-temporal modeling and implement domain-specific video intelligence using techniques inspired by NVIDIA VSS to build robust, life cycle-aware platforms for real-world applications like brand safety and contextual advertising. Explore new solutions for accelerating large-scale video ingestion with modern multi-modal tooling like NVIDIA NeMo Curator, and discover strategies for achieving high-throughput inference through embedding-level optimization and evolving to next-generation, end-to-end validation frameworks. We'll present proven methodologies with customer success examples from enterprises like Samsung Electronics, LG Electronics, and Kenvue to help you deploy enterprise-grade, trustworthy AI systems that analyze video content at scale.

### The Physics of Long-Context Inference: Breaking the Memory Wall With NVIDIA Dynamo (Presented by VAST Data)
Speakers: Alon Horev (Vast Data, Inc.), Anat Heilper (VAST Data)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82255/
Type: Talk
Long-context AI conversations create a "memory wall" where GPU high-bandwidth memory becomes a bottleneck for scaling inference during agentic AI. This session shares a practical study of how NVIDIA Dynamo’s KV Block Manager (KVBM) accelerates large scale inference. We'll examine Dynamo’s four-tiered architecture (G1–G4) that places data in the right tier at the right time to balance higher concurrency, throughput, and cost. We’ll show how VAST Data as the G3 tier achieves maximum memory and storage efficiency to eliminate the memory wall for long-running agents. Finally we’ll dive into the new NVIDIA Inference Context Management Storage platform, which provides a new class of AI-native storage infrastructure designed for gigascale inference. You'll leave with blueprints for architecting secure, persistent memory for the next generation of agentic AI.

### Unlock Sovereign AI With Confidential RAG and Secure Inference for Regulated Data
Speakers: Aaron Fulkerson (Opaque Systems)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82299/
Type: Talk
In this session, we showcase a real-world national deployment that enables confidential retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) and AI inference on protected data—without exposing those data to cloud operators, infrastructure administrators, model providers, or third-party vendors. Learn how NVIDIA Confidential Computing, along with the combined technology from OPAQUE’s Confidential AI Platform and the TII, the applied research arm of the Advanced Technology Research Council (ATRC), establishes hardware-backed trusted execution environments that deliver cryptographic guarantees of data confidentiality, integrity, and verifiable governance across the entire AI life cycle. This architecture enables real-time AI inference and analytics directly on encrypted data, unlocking high-value AI use cases while preserving zero-trust security boundaries.

### Don’t Leave Tensors on the Table: Programming and Optimizing Tensor Cores
Speakers: Magnus Strengert (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81772/
Type: Talk
Explore practical techniques for maximizing Tensor Core performance in your CUDA kernels. We’ll discuss CUTLASS-based coding patterns to unlock efficient pipeline utilization and streamlined data management. Together, we’ll use Nsight Compute to analyze, optimize, and fine-tune your workflows for optimal Tensor Core performance.

### GPU Access to Unbounded Dataset Sizes Breaks Open Document Ingestion and Search
Speakers: CJ Newburn (NVIDIA), Vikram Sharma Mailthody (NVIDIA), Corey Nolet (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81840/
Type: Talk
Vector databases with 1 trillion vectors cannot fit in memory, and VecDB ingestion and search requires the massive concurrency, memory bandwidth, and latency tolerance of GPUs. Come see new programming models, new storage server reference designs, new SSD offerings, and new customer-oriented scaled results.

### How AI Systems Evolve Into Long-Horizon Agents
Speakers: Kay Zhu (Genspark)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82413/
Type: Talk
Learn how an AI system can evolve from simple search into long-horizon agents that run for hours or days to complete complex, real-world tasks. This session walks through four stages of that evolution—AI search, asynchronous workflows, agents, and long-horizon agents—and examines the architectural patterns and technical trade-offs required at each step. We will cover practical approaches we learned building Genspark, including model routing, tool execution frameworks, validation of agent trajectories, and reliability strategies needed to operate agent systems at scale.

### Interactive Digital Twin for Fusion With Integrated Sensors and Agentic AI
Speakers: Raffi Nazikian (General Atomics)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81875/
Type: Talk
The session will describe the development of the digital twin for the DIII-D Experimental Fusion Reactor. The talk will describe the development of the AI models and live sensor data integrated with the CAD data to develop an interactive digital twin.

### Measuring Brand, Fame, and Virality Using Spatio-Temporal Causal Graphs
Speakers: Tomás Puig (Alembic), George Mathew (Alembic)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81594/
Type: Talk
We introduce a GPU-accelerated system for decomposing engagement into Brand (persistent equity), Fame (spatial reach), and Virality (temporal dynamics). Standard analysis cannot separate these effects because they are mathematically entangled: observing any two determines the third. We address this by using spatio-temporal causal graphs that explicitly encode influence structure, with CUDA kernels for CSR propagation and distributed Shapley computation across GPU clusters. Built on an Online Evolving Spike Neural Network distributed pipeline, the system uses variance-optimal stratified sampling for coalition evaluation and processes 100,000+ marketing time series at daily resolution via custom CUDA kernels and C++ simulation bridges. The framework enables teams to identify whether observed variation is driven by content quality, audience segmentation, or timing effects—separating forces that traditional attribution methods conflate. This turns engagement decomposition into actionable budget allocation grounded in large-scale causal inference.

### Operationalizing AI at Scale: NVIDIA’s End‑to‑End Journey to an Enterprise AI Factory
Speakers: Nic Borensztein (NVIDIA), Rama Akkiraju (NVIDIA), Ashwin Jha (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82452/
Type: Talk
Learn how NVIDIA’s IT organization engineered an on‑premises AI factory to deliver agentic AI with enterprise‑grade security, reliability, and governance. We’ll walk through the end-to-end technical stack behind NVIDIA’s internal AI platform, from our on-prem enterprise AI factory infrastructure to containerized agentic applications. This includes scalable inference, secure enterprise data access, agent development and evaluation, standardized agent interaction, memory, runtime execution, and data flywheels for continuous improvement. You’ll see how NVIDIA has deployed AI agents for the chip design, IT, supply chain, and developer and employee productivity domains.

### Physical AI for Healthcare Robotics—Part 2: Closing the Sim-to-Real Gap and Real-World Deployment
Speakers: Patrick Thornycroft (CMR Surgical), Jay Carlson (NVIDIA), Tao Li (MicroPort MedBot (Group) Co., Ltd), Pooria Kashani (Horizon Surgical Systems)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81822/
Type: Lightning Talk
Join Part 2 of this two-part curated session on what it takes to bring simulation-trained robotics into the real world. This session looks at how teams are translating behaviors developed in simulation into reliable clinical performance. We’ll cover practical approaches to closing the sim-to-real gap, along with the real challenges of deploying safe, robust, low-latency sensor-to-action systems in live healthcare environments.

### Standardize Gen AI Service Evaluation: An API-Centric Benchmarking Approach
Speakers: David Kanter (MLCommons)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82276/
Type: Talk
Evaluating Gen AI services across heterogeneous environments presents significant visibility gaps for developers, as traditional hardware-centric benchmarks often fail to reflect production-level API performance and accuracy. This session introduces MLPerf Endpoints, an open-source framework by MLCommons designed to address these challenges through transparent, reproducible, and vendor-neutral benchmarking. By shifting the evaluative focus to the API layer, this methodology enables developers to conduct iterative, on-demand measurements that mirror real-world user experiences and infrastructure optimizations. You'll analyze early cross-provider results and explore visualization tools designed to drive data-driven decisions in AI procurement and system design.

### Workflow Revolution: How Doudna Drives HPC, AI, and Quantum Scientific Impact
Speakers: Rollin Thomas (NERSC, Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81634/
Type: Talk
This presentation will focus on how scientists can leverage the Doudna supercomputer, to be deployed in 2027 at the National Energy Research Scientific Computing Center (NERSC), to manage and execute complex workflows, particularly those involving data sourced from external scientific experiments. We'll explore the system’s architecture, including the integration of Rubin GPUs, and detail how Doudna’s advanced workflow-centric capabilities accelerate science. We will discuss the relevance of these workflows for cutting-edge fields such as AI, modeling and simulation, and quantum computing, and how these new workloads enhance throughput and support of novel scientific discoveries. Gain insight into what goes into designing an HPC system to support these diverse workflows.

### Life Sciences: Scale Multi‑Agent for Accelerated Clinical Operation Systems With NVIDIA and AWS (Presented by Cognizant)
Speakers: Derek Debe (Abbvie), Gaurav Josan (Clario)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82364/
Type: Theater Talk
Discover how life sciences leaders are transforming enterprise‑scale clinical operations through multi‑agent AI systems built on NVIDIA’s accelerated computing and AWS’ cloud‑native intelligence. This session brings AbbVie and Clario to the stage to share how organizations can strategically modernize workflows, unify data and automation platforms, and unlock new operating leverage—positioning their enterprises for faster innovation, responsible AI adoption, and measurable business impact.

### Private AI: Run Modern Models On-Prem Without Moving Sensitive Data (Presented by Cirrascale)
Speakers: Dave Driggers (Cirrascale Cloud Services)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82378/
Type: Theater Talk
Explore why more enterprises are shifting AI workloads closer to protect sensitive company data and how to operationalize Private AI without sacrificing performance. In this session, we’ll break down the real-world drivers behind on-prem and private deployments (data sovereignty, IP protection, regulated workloads, and risk reduction), then walk through a practical blueprint for running modern models with full control of data. Attendees will leave with a clear framework for model selection, secure deployment design, and operational controls so teams can confidently deploy high-performance inference in private environments while keeping sensitive data in place and minimizing exposure.

### Make AI Factories Production-Ready: Inference, Networking, and DPUs (Presented by F5, Inc.)
Speakers: Gregory Dalle (F5, Inc.)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82385/
Type: Theater Talk
As AI factories move from experimentation to production, teams quickly learn that GPU performance alone does not determine inference capacity, latency, or user experience. Inference workloads produce long-lived, bursty, stateful traffic that strains traditional networking and security architectures, reducing token throughput, increasing time-to-first-token, and introducing instability at scale. This session explores how AI factories built on NVIDIA accelerated computing benefit from BlueField DPU-accelerated networking and cloud-native traffic management. We examine the unique demands of AI inference, how NVIDIA DPUs offload and isolate critical data paths, and how inference-aware traffic control improves performance, reduces latency, and enables consistent multi-tenant inference services in production environments.

### Making AI Agents Explainable for Manufacturing at Scale With Dataiku and NVIDIA (Presented by Dataiku)
Speakers: Hannes Hapke (Dataiku)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82365/
Type: Theater Talk
As manufacturers accelerate toward autonomous AI agents to reduce costly unplanned downtime, trust, transparency, and safety have become the critical blockers to adoption in high-stakes environments. This session shows how Dataiku and NVIDIA, together with the Puffin Inspector framework from Dataiku’s Open Source Lab, make AI-driven scheduling explainable, governed, and ready for real-world industrial orchestration today.

### Designing Modular Infrastructure for the AI Factory Era (Presented by Intelliflex)
Speakers: Charles L'Ecuyer (Intelliflex)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82343/
Type: Theater Talk
AI workloads are scaling faster than traditional data center models were designed to support, particularly as power density requirements increase and expansion timelines compress. In this session, we explore the infrastructure requirements of AI factories—including power density, modular deployment approaches, and direct liquid cooling readiness—and examine architectural strategies that enable phased scalability while avoiding the constraints of retrofitting legacy, low-density facilities for accelerated computing at scale.

### Liquid Cooling Readiness for Vera-Rubin Reference Architectures (Presented by nVent)
Speakers: Matthew Archibald (nVent)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82328/
Type: Theater Talk
Vera-Rubin deployments extend the performance envelope of direct-to-chip liquid cooling while remaining compatible with the same core liquid-cooling architecture used for Grace-Blackwell systems. This session defines what it means to be “liquid-cooling ready” for NVIDIA SuperPOD reference architectures by examining coolant distribution unit (CDU) and technology cooling system (TCS) network design across Grace-Blackwell and Vera-Rubin platforms. A properly designed TCS manifold with common CDU and rack-drop interfaces enables scalability through CDU count and rack-drop configuration, allowing systems to scale from GB200 to Vera-Rubin without changes to the underlying TCS architecture.

### From Smart Factory to Lights-Out Factory: Operationalizing Industrial AI With LTTS and NVIDIA (Presented by LTTS)
Speakers: Umashankar KA (L&T Technology Services Limited)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82377/
Type: Theater Talk
Manufacturing is advancing automation, yet factories still depend on human input for planning, quality, and operations. The next step is autonomous, self-optimizing factories powered by industrial AI. L&T Technology Services (LTTS) introduces its Lights-Out Factory (LoF) solution, built on NVIDIA’s Industrial AI platform, Omniverse, Isaac, and CUDA-accelerated AI. LoF integrates digital twins, robotics, and AI-driven decision-making into a closed-loop system, enabling factories to move from assisted automation to autonomy. This session shows how LTTS combines manufacturing expertise with NVIDIA’s AI to create learning factories. Discover how virtual twins simulate workflows, AI adapts to real-time conditions, and robotics improve yield, safety, and throughput. Real-world results include reduced cycle times, improved yields, and faster ramp-ups, with a roadmap for scaling LoF deployments

### How AI-Infused Platforms Redefine Modernization, Delivery, and Value (Presented by Wipro)
Speakers: Shivakumar Krishnan (Wipro)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-ex82326/
Type: Theater Talk
In the AI era, competitiveness will be determined not by how fast enterprises modernize once, but by how effectively they institutionalize change itself. AI platforms illustrate this shift by productizing modernization intelligence, embedding AI across the software life cycle, and enabling enterprises to reduce the cost of change while accelerating AI readiness.

### AI Coding for the GPU: Build a Coding Agent to Help GPU Developers Write Speed-of-Light Code
Speakers: Matt Frazier (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81831/
Type: Talk
Discover the evolution of AI-assisted development for accelerated computing as we move beyond general-purpose coding assistants to specialized agents designed for high-performance CUDA optimization. While frontier models have improved at syntax correctness, they often lack the domain-specific context required to utilize the latest hardware features and achieve "speed of light" performance. This session details NVIDIA’s "CUDA Intelligence" ecosystem—a transition from simple retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) pipelines to advanced agentic workflows using the model context protocol (MCP) and agentic orchestration. We will demonstrate how we are democratizing state-of-the-art code transformation by integrating cloud profiling, Nsight tools, and expert-level optimization skills into a constellation of AI services that can run locally or in the cloud.

### Authorship Reimagined: AI, Art, and the Future of Creative Expression
Speakers: Louis Stewart (NVIDIA), Ia Robinson (Curious Refuge), Vincent Brisebois (NVIDIA), Jason Harvey (BET+), Paul Trillo (Asteria)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82121/
Type: Panel
A new era of storytelling is unfolding, where artists and intelligent systems co-create, challenge tradition, and push the boundaries of expression. This panel brings together leaders at the intersection of art, education, and technology, including the creative visionaries shaping the next frontier of cinema, design, and digital experience. From generative animation tools built for Hollywood to AI frameworks informing visual storytelling, these panelists are not asking whether AI belongs in the creative process but how to wield it with intention, imagination, and integrity. Together, they will explore what authorship means when machines become part of the medium, how education is evolving to prepare tomorrow's creators, and why protecting creative agency matters more than ever in an AI-powered world. This is a conversation about design and disruption, culture and code, where the future of art is not automated but amplified.

### Build, Optimize, Run: The Developer's Guide to Local Gen AI on NVIDIA RTX AI PCs
Speakers: Annamalai Chockalingam (NVIDIA), Parth Sareen (Ollama)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82128/
Type: Talk
The AI PC ecosystem is exploding. Developers are now running local, high-performance AI workloads. This technical session dives into how NVIDIA accelerates the top open-source software stack—from the different quantization techniques, training recipes, as well as inference frameworks and tools like Ollama, ComfyUI and custom pipelines. Crucially, we will move beyond simple inference to address the architecture of reliable, local agentic workflows. Join us to learn about the technical intricacies and considerations to develop robust, local AI workflows on NVIDIA RTX.

### Hardware-Efficient and Lossless Video Generation at Scale: Native Sparse Attention and Mixed-Low-Bit Quantization
Speakers: Guangyun Han (NVIDIA), Yiqiao Liao (Kuaishou)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81693/
Type: Tutorial
We present a unified, hardware-aware framework for scaling large video generation models—such as Diffusion Transformers (DiT)—without compromising visual fidelity. Learn how production-grade model compression techniques jointly eliminate the quadratic cost bottlenecks that limit training for long-context AIGC models. The session covers domain-specialized sparse attention for video sequences, robust low-precision training pipelines especially about low-bit attention mechanism. With industrial-scale results demonstrating 2x end-to-end acceleration from sparse attention and 60–70% savings via low-bit computation—all with near-zero accuracy loss—this talk provides a practical blueprint for building efficient, lossless, and deployable video generative models at scale.

### How to Build End‑to‑End Physical AI Systems for Humanoid Robots
Speakers: Akul Santhosh (NVIDIA), Edith Llontop (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81478/
Type: Tutorial
Physical AI brings intelligence to machines, industrial arms, autonomous mobile robots (AMRs), and humanoids, enabling them to perceive, reason, and act in the real world in real time. These robots go beyond scripted behavior to make intelligent decisions on the fly: moving materials, assembling parts, and collaborating safely with people in dynamic environments. In this session, experts will walk through practical, end-to-end workflows for physical AI development using NVIDIA Isaac and Omniverse technologies. You’ll learn workflows spanning data collection and curation, digital twin creation, model training and scaling, high-fidelity simulation, and deployment to real hardware. We’ll highlight proven toolchains and real-world examples that developers can apply to accelerate the path to smarter, more adaptable robots.

### Parameterized CUDA Graph Launch in PyTorch: CUDA Graphs Without the Pain
Speakers: Daniel Galvez (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81709/
Type: Talk
Modern GPUs have gotten so fast that CPUs cannot launch new work to the GPUs fast enough, which reduces overall efficiency. CUDA Graphs can eliminate CPU overhead completely, but have traditionally been hard to use, so let’s break down the problems and fixes for them, focusing on PyTorch in particular.

### Solve the Day 1 Challenge of Landing LLM Inference in Enterprise
Speakers: Tongyu Guo (Alibaba Cloud Intelligence), Kai Zhang (Alibaba Cloud Intelligence)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81462/
Type: Talk
Alibaba Cloud ACK RBG is a management tool for the full life cycle of large model inference deployment on Kubernetes, designed to address the challenges of large-scale deployment and operations of prefill-decode (PD) disaggregation architecture within Kubernetes clusters. It not only provides features such as multi-role management/collaboration, auto service discovery, topology-aware placement, elastic scaling, and atomic rollout/failure recovery, but also tightly integrates with NVIDIA Dynamo's AIConfigurator and Planner to achieve performance profiling and smarter scaling in complex PD disaggregation and mixture-of-experts (MoE) scenarios. This aims to further improve inference efficiency and reduce costs while meeting service level agreement (SLA) requirements, representing one of the effective ways for large-scale practical deployment of NVIDIA Dynamo.

### Break Communication Barriers: Scale AI and HPC with NCCL, NIXL, and NVSHMEM
Speakers: Matthew Nicely (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82407/
Type: Talk
Learn how to eliminate communication bottlenecks with NVIDIA Communication stack. Over the past year, each library has evolved to address specific bottlenecks: NCCL introduced Symmetric Memory support and Copy Engine collectives, enabling zero-SM communication. NVSHMEM enhanced GPU-initiated operations in the Python ecosystem. NIXL emerged as the inference-focused solution, providing async, noncontiguous transfers across memory tiers (GPU HBM, CPU DRAM, local/networked storage) with backends including GDS, UCX, and S3—essential for disaggregated inference architectures. Explore developer optimizations: custom collectives in NCCL, KV cache and storage innovations in NIXL, and expert parallelism patterns. Learn how Spectrum-X plugins extend capabilities across multi-site deployments for scale-across connectivity.

### Build a Software-Defined Multi-Tenant NVLinked Cluster
Speakers: Adam Tetelman (NVIDIA), Alex Saroyan (Netris), Lukas Gentele (vCluster Labs)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81640/
Type: Talk
AI workloads are scaling into full production for training and inference. Standing up these clusters for R&D is challenging on its own, but production brings complexity: stricter security requirements, operational rigor, and a broader user base demanding stability, flexibility, and mature features. The solution is a single, enterprise-grade, software-defined NVL GPU cluster built to dynamically adapt to changing workloads, support rapid deployment, and simplify life-cycle management. In this talk, we’ll examine considerations across the infrastructure, platform, and software layers. We’ll look at network automation, virtualization, and different orchestration platforms. We’ll focus with a concrete example based on our reference architecture, integrating Run:ai, Base Command Manager, vCluster, and Netris to create a secure, scalable, production-ready multi-tenant cluster.

### Expression About Expression: When AI Looks in the Mirror
Speakers: Shari Reich (NVIDIA), Grimes (KNGMKR), Matt Zien (KNGMKR)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82451/
Type: Panel
Despite fears of replacement, we may be entering a new wave of human creativity. AI systems increasingly generate content that comments on themselves or reveals emergent behavior. What happens when human judgment and machine intelligence blend together? This panel looks at emerging forms of expression that arise from human–AI collaboration, including outputs that feel unfamiliar or self‑reflective.

### Orchestrate Next-Generation AI Workloads With Open-Source Slurm
Speakers: Tim Wickberg (NVIDIA), Danny Auble (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82420/
Type: Talk
Scaling next-generation discovery requires more than increased computational power; it also depends on flexible, reliable workload orchestration at large scale. This session examines how Slurm can be used to manage traditional HPC jobs, Kubernetes-based AI workloads with complex topology requirements, and converged environments in a single ecosystem. We will outline the current architecture, recent enhancements, and ongoing community-driven work that target higher efficiency, portability, and interoperability for supercomputing workloads over the coming decade.

### Top-K Selection at the Speed of Light
Speakers: Christina Zhang (NVIDIA), Yue Weng (NVIDIA), Elias Stehle (NVIDIA)
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s81614/
Type: Talk
Explore new techniques for top-k selection, a critical operation for accelerating scientific computing and mixture of experts that efficiently extracts the most relevant items from massive datasets. Dive into the design of our state-of-the-art GPU algorithm, cub::DeviceTopK, and learn how leveraging this primitive can accelerate high-throughput workloads by over 160x compared to previous methods.

### Build-a-Claw Event
page: https://www.nvidia.com/gtc/session-catalog/sessions/gtc26-s82795/
Type: Talk
Stop by NVIDIA’s build-a-claw event in the GTC Park running all week to customize and deploy a proactive, always-on AI assistant. With support from NVIDIA experts, GTC attendees can quickly have an AI agent at their fingertips. Whether technical or just curious, participants will name an agent, define its personality and grant it access to the tools it needs. Think of it as the personal assistant you’ve always wanted, reachable via your preferred messaged app. To run the custom agent, bring your own NVIDIA DGX Spark. Or buy one at the Gear Store (SJCC street level) or MicroCenter pop-up (GTC Park). To run the custom agent, use cloud compute provided onsite, or harness local accelerated computing by bringing your NVIDIA DGX Spark or GeForce RTX laptop, with no personal data on the device. NVIDIA hardware — including DGX Spark — will also be available to buy on site at Gear Store (SJCC street level) and Micro Center (pop-up at GTC Park).
